| SECOND SOLDIER | Was ever father so bemoaned his son? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Was ever [[archetypes/king\|king]] so grieved for subjects’ woe?<br>Much is your sorrow, mine ten times so much. |
| FIRST SOLDIER | (to his father’s [[concepts/body\|body]])<br>I’ll bear thee hence where I may weep my fill. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit at one door with the [[concepts/body\|body]] of his father |
| SECOND SOLDIER | (to his son’s [[concepts/body\|body]])<br>These arms of mine shall be thy winding sheet;<br>My heart, sweet boy, shall be thy sepulchre,<br>For from my heart thine image ne‘er shall go.<br>My sighing breast shall be thy funeral bell,<br>And so obsequious will thy father be,<br>E’en for the loss of thee, having no more,<br>As Priam was for all his valiant sons.<br>I’ll bear thee hence, and let them fight that will—<br>For I have murdered where I should not kill. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit at another door with the [[concepts/body\|body]] of his son |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Sad-hearted men, much overgone with care,<br>Here sits a [[archetypes/king\|king]] more woeful than you are. |
| *(didascalia)* | Alarums. Excursions. Enter Prince Edward |
| PRINCE EDWARD | Fly, father, fly—for all your friends are fled,<br>And Warwick rages like a chafed bull!<br>Away—for [[motifs/death\|death]] doth hold us in pursuit! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]] |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Mount you, my lord—towards Berwick post amain.<br>Edward and [[characters/richard\|Richard]], like a brace of greyhounds<br>Having the fearful flying hare in sight,<br>With fiery eyes sparkling for very wrath,<br>And bloody steel grasped in their ireful hands,<br>Are at our backs—and therefore hence amain. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Exeter |
| EXETER | Away—for vengeance comes along with them!<br>Nay—stay not to expostulate—make [[characters/speed\|speed]]—<br>Or else come after. I’ll away before. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Nay, take me with thee, good sweet Exeter.<br>Not that I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] to stay, but [[motifs/love\|love]] to go<br>Whither the [[characters/queen\|Queen]] intends. Forward, away. Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 6

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | A loud alarum. Enter Lord [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]], wounded with an arrow in his neck |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | Here burns my candle out—ay, here it dies,<br>Which, whiles it lasted, gave [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry light.<br>O Lancaster, I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] thy overthrow<br>More than my [[concepts/body\|body]]’s parting with my [[motifs/soul\|soul]]!<br>My [[motifs/love\|love]] and [[concepts/fear\|fear]] glued many friends to thee—<br>And, now I fall, thy tough commixture melts,<br>Impairing Henry, strength’ning misproud York.<br>The common people swarm like [[motifs/summer\|summer]] flies,<br>And whither fly the gnats but to the sun?<br>And who shines now but Henry’s enemies?<br>O Phoebus, hadst thou never given consent<br>That Phaeton should check thy fiery steeds,<br>Thy burning car never had scorched the earth!<br>And, Henry, hadst thou swayed as kings should do,<br>Or as thy father and his father did,<br>Giving no ground unto the house of York,<br>They never then had sprung like [[motifs/summer\|summer]] flies;<br>I and ten thousand in this luckless realm<br>Had left no mourning widows for our [[motifs/death\|death]];<br>And thou this day hadst kept thy chair in peace.<br>For what doth cherish weeds, but gentle air?<br>And what makes robbers bold, but too much lenity?<br>Bootless are plaints, and cureless are my wounds;<br>No way to fly, nor strength to hold out flight;<br>The foe is merciless and will not pity,<br>For at their hands I have deserved no pity.<br>The air hath got into my deadly wounds,<br>And much effuse of [[motifs/blood\|blood]] doth make me faint.<br>Come York and [[characters/richard\|Richard]], Warwick and the rest—<br>I stabbed your fathers’ bosoms; split my breast. |
| *(didascalia)* | He faints. |
| *(didascalia)* | Alarum and retreat. Enter Edward [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]], |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | his brothers George and [[characters/richard\|Richard]], the Earl of |
| *(didascalia)* | Warwick, the Marquis of Montague, and soldiers |
| EDWARD | Now breathe we, lords—good fortune bids us pause,<br>And smooth the frowns of war with peaceful looks.<br>Some troops pursue the bloody-minded [[characters/queen\|Queen]],<br>That led calm Henry, though he were a [[archetypes/king\|king]],<br>As doth a sail filled with a fretting gust<br>Command an argosy to stem the waves.<br>But think you, lords, that [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]] fled with them? |
| WARWICK | No—‘tis impossible he should escape;<br>For, though before his face I speak the words,<br>Your brother [[characters/richard\|Richard]] marked him for the [[motifs/grave\|grave]].<br>And whereso’er he is, he’s surely dead.<br>[[characters/clifford\|Clifford]] groans |
| *(didascalia)* | EDWARD |
| WARWICK | Whose [[motifs/soul\|soul]] is that which takes her heavy leave? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] |
| WARWICK | A deadly groan, like life and [[motifs/death\|death]]’s departing. |
| *(didascalia)* | EDWARD to [[characters/richard\|Richard]] |
| WARWICK | See who it is. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/richard\|Richard]] goes to [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]] |
| WARWICK | And now the battle’s ended,<br>If friend or foe, let him be gently used. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] | Revoke that doom of [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]], for ‘tis [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]];<br>Who not contented that he lopped the branch<br>In hewing Rutland when his [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]] put forth,<br>But set his murd’ring knife unto the root<br>From whence that tender spray did sweetly [[motifs/spring\|spring]]—<br>I mean our princely father, [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]]. |
| WARWICK | From off the gates of York fetch down the head,<br>Your father’s head, which [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]] placed there.<br>Instead whereof let this supply the room—<br>Measure for measure must be answerèd. |
| EDWARD | Bring forth that fatal screech-owl to our house,<br>That nothing sung but [[motifs/death\|death]] to us and ours. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]] is dragged forward |
| EDWARD | Now [[motifs/death\|death]] shall stop his dismal threat’ning sound<br>And his ill-boding tongue no more shall speak. |
| WARWICK | I think his understanding is bereft.<br>Speak, [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]], dost thou know who speaks to thee?<br>Dark cloudy [[motifs/death\|death]] o’ershades his beams of life,<br>And he nor sees nor hears us what we say. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] | O, would he did—and so perhaps he doth.<br>’Tis but his policy to counterfeit,<br>Because he would avoid such bitter taunts<br>Which in the [[motifs/time\|time]] of [[motifs/death\|death]] he gave our father. |
| GEORGE | If so thou think’st, vex him with eager words. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] | [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]], ask [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] and obtain no grace. |
| EDWARD | [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]], repent in bootless penitence. |
| WARWICK | [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]], devise excuses for thy faults. |
| GEORGE | While we devise fell tortures for thy faults. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] | Thou didst [[motifs/love\|love]] York, and I am son to York. |
| EDWARD | Thou pitied’st Rutland—I will pity thee. |
| GEORGE | Where’s Captain Margaret to fence you now? |
| WARWICK | They mock thee, [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]]—swear as thou wast wont. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] | What, not an oath? Nay, then, the world goes hard<br>When [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]] cannot spare his friends an oath.<br>I know by that he’s dead—and, by my [[motifs/soul\|soul]],<br>If this right hand would buy but two hours’ life<br>That I, in all despite, might rail at him,<br>This hand should chop it off, and with the issuing<br>[[motifs/blood\|blood]]<br>Stifle the [[archetypes/villain\|villain]] whose unstanchèd thirst<br>York and young Rutland could not satisfy. |
| WARWICK | Ay, but he’s dead. Off with the traitor’s head,<br>And rear it in the place your father’s stands.<br>And now to [[settings/london\|London]] with triumphant march,<br>There to be crowned England’s royal [[archetypes/king\|king]];<br>From whence shall Warwick cut the sea to France,<br>And ask the Lady Bona for thy [[characters/queen\|queen]].<br>So shalt thou sinew both these lands together.<br>And, having France thy friend, thou shalt not dread<br>The scattered foe that hopes to rise again,<br>For though they cannot greatly sting to hurt,<br>Yet look to have them buzz to offend thine ears.<br>First will I see the coronation,<br>And then to Brittany I’ll [[motifs/cross\|cross]] the sea<br>To effect this [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]], so it please my lord. |
| EDWARD | Even as thou wilt, sweet Warwick, let it be.<br>For in thy shoulder do I build my seat,<br>And never will I undertake the thing<br>Wherein thy counsel and consent is wanting.<br>[[characters/richard\|Richard]], I will create thee [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]],<br>And George, of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]]; Warwick, as ourself,<br>Shall do and undo as him pleaseth best. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] | Let me be Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], George of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]]—<br>For [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]]’s dukedom is too ominous. |
| WARWICK | Tut, that’s a foolish observation—<br>[[characters/richard\|Richard]], be [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]]. Now to [[settings/london\|London]]<br>To see these honours in possession. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt. York’s head is removed |

### Atto 3, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter two Gamekeepers, with crossbows in their hands |
| FIRST GAMEKEEPER | Under this thick-grown brake we’ll shroud ourselves,<br>For through this laund anon the deer will come,<br>And in this covert will we make our stand,<br>Culling the principal of all the deer. |
| SECOND GAMEKEEPER | I’ll stay above the [[motifs/hill\|hill]], so both may shoot. |
| FIRST GAMEKEEPER | That cannot be—the noise of thy crossbow<br>Will scare the herd, and so my shoot is lost.<br>Here stand we both, and aim we at the best.<br>And, for the [[motifs/time\|time]] shall not seem tedious,<br>I’ll tell thee what befell me on a day<br>In this [[concepts/self\|self]] place where now we mean to stand. |
| FIRST GAMEKEEPER | Here comes a man—let’s stay till he be past.<br>They stand apart. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry, disguised, carrying a [[motifs/prayer\|prayer]]-book |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | From Scotland am I stolen, even of pure [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>To greet mine own land with my wishful sight.<br>No, Harry, Harry—’tis no land of thine.<br>Thy place is filled, thy sceptre wrung from thee,<br>Thy balm washed off wherewith thou wast anointed.<br>No bending knee will call thee Caesar now,<br>No humble suitors press to speak for right,<br>No, not a man comes for redress of thee—<br>For how can I help them and not myself? |
| FIRST GAMEKEEPER | (to the Second Gamekeeper)<br>Ay, here’s a deer whose skin’s a keeper’s fee:<br>This is the quondam [[archetypes/king\|king]]—let’s seize upon him. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Let me embrace thee, sour adversity,<br>For wise men say it is the wisest course. |
| SECOND GAMEKEEPER | (to the First Gamekeeper)<br>Why linger we? Let us lay hands upon him. |
| FIRST GAMEKEEPER | (to the Second Gamekeeper)<br>Forbear awhile—we’ll hear a little more. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | My [[characters/queen\|queen]] and son are gone to France for aid,<br>And, as I hear, the great commanding Warwick<br>Is thither gone to crave the French [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s sister<br>To wife for Edward. If this news be true,<br>Poor [[characters/queen\|Queen]] and son, your labour is but lost—<br>For Warwick is a subtle orator,<br>And Louis a prince soon won with moving words.<br>By this account, then, Margaret may win him—<br>For she’s a woman to be pitied much.<br>Her sighs will make a batt‘ry in his breast,<br>Her [[motifs/tears\|tears]] will pierce into a marble heart,<br>The tiger will be mild whiles she doth mourn,<br>And Nero will be tainted with remorse<br>To hear and see her plaints, her brinish [[motifs/tears\|tears]].<br>Ay, but she’s come to beg; Warwick to give.<br>She on his left side, craving aid for Henry;<br>He on his right, asking a wife for Edward.<br>She weeps and says her Henry is deposed,<br>He smiles and says his Edward is installed;<br>That she, poor wretch, for grief can speak no more,<br>Whiles Warwick tells his title, smooths the wrong,<br>Inferreth arguments of mighty strength,<br>And in conclusion wins the [[archetypes/king\|King]] from her<br>With promise of his sister and what else<br>To strengthen and support [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward’s place.<br>O, Margaret, thus ’twill be; and thou, poor [[motifs/soul\|soul]],<br>Art then forsaken, as thou went’st forlorn. |
| SECOND GAMEKEEPER | (coming orward)<br>Say, what art thou that talk’st of kings and queens? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | More than I seem, and less than I was born to:<br>A man at least, for less I should not be;<br>And men may talk of kings, and why not I? |
| SECOND GAMEKEEPER | Ay, but thou talk’st as if thou wert a [[archetypes/king\|king]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Why, so I am, in mind—and that’s enough. |
| SECOND GAMEKEEPER | But if thou be a [[archetypes/king\|king]], where is thy [[motifs/crown\|crown]]? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | My [[motifs/crown\|crown]] is in my heart, not on my head;<br>Not decked with diamonds and Indian stones,<br>Nor to be seen. My [[motifs/crown\|crown]] is called content—<br>A [[motifs/crown\|crown]] it is that seldom kings enjoy. |
| SECOND GAMEKEEPER | [[motifs/well\|Well]], if you be a [[archetypes/king\|king]] crowned with content,<br>Your [[motifs/crown\|crown]] content and you must be contented<br>To go along with us—for, as we think,<br>You are the [[archetypes/king\|king]] [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward hath deposed,<br>And we his subjects sworn in all allegiance<br>Will apprehend you as his enemy. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | But did you never swear and break an oath? |
| SECOND GAMEKEEPER | No—never such an oath, nor will not now. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Where did you dwell when I was [[archetypes/king\|King]] of England? |
| SECOND GAMEKEEPER | Here in this country, where we now remain. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | I was anointed [[archetypes/king\|king]] at nine months old,<br>My father and my grandfather were kings,<br>And you were sworn true subjects unto me—<br>And tell me, then, have you not broke your oaths ? |
| FIRST GAMEKEEPER | No, for we were subjects but while you were [[archetypes/king\|king]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Why, am I dead? Do I not breathe a man?<br>Ah, simple men, you know not what you swear.<br>Look as I blow this feather from my face,<br>And as the air blows it to me again,<br>Obeying with my [[motifs/wind\|wind]] when I do blow,<br>And yielding to another when it blows,<br>Commanded always by the greater gust—<br>Such is the lightness of you common men.<br>But do not break your oaths, for of that sin<br>My mild entreaty shall not make you guilty.<br>Go where you will, the [[archetypes/king\|King]] shall be commanded;<br>And be you kings, command, and I’ll obey. |
| FIRST GAMEKEEPER | We are true subjects to the [[archetypes/king\|King]], [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | So would you be again to Henry,<br>If he were seated as [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward is. |
| FIRST GAMEKEEPER | We charge you, in God’s name and in the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s,<br>To go with us unto the officers. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | In God’s name, lead; your [[archetypes/king\|king]]’s name be obeyed;<br>And what God will, that let your [[archetypes/king\|king]] perform;<br>And what he will I humbly yield unto. Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward, [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], and the Lady Gray |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Brother of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]], at Saint Albans field<br>This lady’s husband, Sir [[characters/richard\|Richard]] Gray, was slain,<br>His lands then seized on by the conqueror.<br>Her suit is now to repossess those lands,<br>Which we in justice cannot [[motifs/well\|well]] deny,<br>Because in quarrel of the house of York<br>The worthy gentleman did lose his life. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Your highness shall do [[motifs/well\|well]] to grant her suit—<br>It were dishonour to deny it her. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | It were no less; but yet I’ll make a pause. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF | [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]], (aside to George) Yea, is it so?<br>I see the lady hath a thing to grant<br>Before the [[archetypes/king\|King]] will grant her humble suit. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | (aside to [[characters/richard\|Richard]])<br>He knows the game; how true he keeps the [[motifs/wind\|wind]]! |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (aside to George) [[motifs/silence\|Silence]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (to Lady Gray)<br>Widow, we will consider of your suit;<br>And come some other [[motifs/time\|time]] to know our mind. |
| LADY GRAY | Right gracious lord, I cannot brook delay.<br>May it please your highness to resolve me now,<br>And what your pleasure is shall satisfy me. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF | [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]], (aside to George)<br>Ay, widow ? Then I’ll warrant you all your lands<br>An if what pleases him shall pleasure you.<br>Fight closer, or, good [[concepts/faith\|faith]], you’ll catch a blow. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | (aside to [[characters/richard\|Richard]])<br>I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] her not unless she chance to fall. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (aside to George)<br>God forbid that! For he’ll take vantages. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (to Lady Gray)<br>How many children hast thou, widow ? Tell me. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | (aside to [[characters/richard\|Richard]])<br>I think he means to beg a [[archetypes/child\|child]] of her. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (aside to George)<br>Nay, whip me then—he’ll rather give her two. |
| LADY GRAY | (to [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward) Three, my most gracious lord. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (aside)<br>You shall have four, an you’ll be ruled by him. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (to Lady Gray)<br>’Twere pity they should lose their father’s lands. |
| LADY GRAY | Be pitiful, dread lord, and grant it them. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (to [[characters/richard\|Richard]] and George)<br>Lords, give us leave—I’ll try this widow’s wit. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]], aside to George |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Ay, good leave have you; for you will have leave,<br>Till youth take leave and leave you to the crutch.<br>[[characters/richard\|Richard]] and George stand apart |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (to Lady Gray)<br>Now tell me, madam, do you [[motifs/love\|love]] your children? |
| LADY GRAY | Ay, full as dearly as I [[motifs/love\|love]] myself. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | And would you not do much to do them good? |
| LADY GRAY | To do them good I would sustain some harm. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Then get your husband’s lands, to do them good. |
| LADY GRAY | Therefore I came unto your majesty. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | I’ll tell you how these lands are to be got. |
| LADY GRAY | So shall you bind me to your highness’ service. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | What service wilt thou do me, if I give them? |
| LADY GRAY | What you command, that rests in me to do. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | But you will take exceptions to my boon. |
| LADY GRAY | No, gracious lord, except I cannot do it. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Ay, but thou canst do what I mean to ask. |
| LADY GRAY | Why, then, I will do what your grace commands. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to George)<br>He plies her hard, and much rain wears the marble. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | As red as [[motifs/fire\|fire]]! Nay, then her wax must melt. |
| LADY GRAY | (to [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward)<br>Why stops my lord? Shall I not hear my task? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | An easy task—’tis but to [[motifs/love\|love]] a [[archetypes/king\|king]]. |
| LADY GRAY | That’s soon performed, because I am a subject. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Why, then, thy husband’s lands I freely give thee. 55 |
| LADY GRAY | (curtsies)<br>I take my leave, with many thousand thanks. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to George)<br>The match is made—she seals it with a curtsy. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (to Lady Gray)<br>But stay thee—’tis the fruits of [[motifs/love\|love]] I mean. |
| LADY GRAY | The fruits of [[motifs/love\|love]] I mean, my loving liege. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Ay, but I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] me in another sense.<br>What [[motifs/love\|love]] think’st thou I sue so much to get? |
| LADY GRAY | My [[motifs/love\|love]] till [[motifs/death\|death]], my humble thanks, my prayers—<br>That [[motifs/love\|love]] which virtue begs and virtue grants. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | No, by my troth, I did not mean such [[motifs/love\|love]]. |
| LADY GRAY | Why, then, you mean not as I thought you did. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | But now you partly may perceive my mind. |
| LADY GRAY | My mind will never grant what I perceive<br>Your highness aims at, if I aim aright. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | To tell thee plain, I aim to lie with thee. |
| LADY GRAY | To tell you plain, I had rather lie in [[motifs/prison\|prison]]. 70 |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Why, then, thou shalt not have thy husband’s lands. |
| LADY GRAY | Why, then, mine honesty shall be my dower;<br>For by that loss I will not purchase them. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Therein thou wrong’st thy children mightily. |
| LADY GRAY | Herein your highness wrongs both them and me.<br>But, mighty lord, this merry inclination<br>Accords not with the sadness of my suit.<br>Please you dismiss me either with ay or no. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Ay, if thou wilt say ‘ay’ to my request;<br>No, if thou dost say ‘no’ to my demand. |
| LADY GRAY | Then, no, my lord—my suit is at an end. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to George)<br>The widow likes him not—she knits her brows. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | He is the bluntest wooer in Christendom. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (aside)<br>Her looks doth argue her replete with modesty;<br>Her words doth show her wit incomparable;<br>All her perfections challenge sovereignty.<br>One way or other, she is for a [[archetypes/king\|king]];<br>And she shall be my [[motifs/love\|love]] or else my [[characters/queen\|queen]].<br>(To Lady Gray) Say that [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward take thee for his<br>[[characters/queen\|queen]]? |
| LADY GRAY | ’Tis better said than done, my gracious lord.<br>I am a subject fit to jest withal,<br>But far unfit to be a sovereign. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Sweet widow, by my state I swear to thee<br>I speak no more than what my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] intends,<br>And that is to enjoy thee for my [[motifs/love\|love]]. |
| LADY GRAY | And that is more than I will yield unto.<br>I know I am too mean to be your [[characters/queen\|queen]],<br>And yet too good to be your concubine. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | You cavil, widow-I did mean my [[characters/queen\|queen]]. |
| LADY GRAY | ’Twill grieve your grace my sons should call you father. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | No more than when my daughters call thee mother.<br>Thou art a widow and thou hast some children;<br>And, by God’s mother, I, being but a bachelor,<br>Have other some. Why, ’tis a happy thing<br>To be the father unto many sons.<br>Answer no more, for thou shalt be my [[characters/queen\|queen]]. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to George)<br>The ghostly father now hath done his shrift. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | When he was made a shriver, ’twas for shift. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (to [[characters/richard\|Richard]] and George)<br>Brothers, you muse what chat we two have had. [[characters/richard\|Richard]] and George come forward |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The widow likes it not, for she looks very sad. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | You’d think it strange if I should marry her. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | To who, my lord ? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Why, [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], to myself. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | That would be ten days’ [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] at the least. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | That’s a day longer than a [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] lasts. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | By so much is the [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] in extremes. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | [[motifs/well\|Well]], jest on, brothers—I can tell you both<br>Her suit is granted for her husband’s lands. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Nobleman |
| NOBLEMAN | My gracious lord, Henry your foe is taken<br>And brought as prisoner to your palace gate. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | See that he be conveyed unto the Tower—<br>(To [[characters/richard\|Richard]] and George)<br><br>And go we, brothers, to the man that took him,<br>To question of his apprehension. |
| *(didascalia)* | (To Lady Gray) Widow, go you along. To [[characters/richard\|Richard]] and |
| *(didascalia)* | George Lords, use her honourably. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[characters/richard\|Richard]] |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF GLOUCEST’ER | Ay, Edward will use women honourably.<br>Would he were wasted, marrow, bones, and all,<br>That from his loins no hopeful branch may [[motifs/spring\|spring]]<br>To [[motifs/cross\|cross]] me from the golden [[motifs/time\|time]] I look for.<br>And yet, between my [[motifs/soul\|soul]]’s [[motifs/desire\|desire]] and me—<br>The lustful Edward’s title burièd—<br>Is [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], Henry, and his son young Edward,<br>And all the unlooked-for issue of their bodies,<br>To take their rooms ere I can place myself.<br>A cold premeditation for my purpose.<br>Why, then, I do but dream on sovereignty<br>Like one that stands upon a promontory<br>And spies a far-off shore where he would tread,<br>Wishing his foot were equal with his eye,<br>And chides the sea that sunders him from thence,<br>Saying he’ll lade it dry to have his way—<br>So do I wish the [[motifs/crown\|crown]] being so far off,<br>And so I chide the means that keeps me from it,<br>And so I say I’ll cut the causes off,<br>Flattering me with impossibilities.<br>My eye’s too quick, my heart o‘erweens too much,<br>Unless my hand and strength could equal them.<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]], say there is no kingdom then for [[characters/richard\|Richard]]—<br>What other pleasure can the world afford?<br>I’ll make my heaven in a lady’s lap,<br>And deck my [[concepts/body\|body]] in gay ornaments,<br>And ’[[characters/witch\|witch]] sweet ladies with my words and looks.<br>O, miserable thought! And more unlikely<br>Than to accomplish twenty golden crowns.<br>Why, [[motifs/love\|love]] forswore me in my mother’s womb,<br>And, for I should not deal in her soft laws,<br>She did corrupt frail [[concepts/nature\|nature]] with some bribe<br>To shrink mine arm up like a withered shrub,<br>To make an envious mountain on my back—<br>Where sits deformity to mock my [[concepts/body\|body]]—<br>To shape my legs of an unequal size,<br>To disproportion me in every part,<br>Like to a chaos, or an unlicked bear whelp<br>That carries no impression like the dam.<br>And am I then a man to be beloved?<br>O, monstrous fault, to harbour such a thought!<br>Then, since this earth affords no joy to me<br>But to command, to check, to o‘erbear such<br>As are of better person than myself,<br>I’ll make my heaven to dream upon the [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>And whiles I live, t’account this world but hell,<br>Until my misshaped trunk that bears this head<br>Be round impaled with a glorious [[motifs/crown\|crown]].<br>And yet I know not how to get the [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>For many lives stand between me and [[motifs/home\|home]].<br>And I—like one lost in a thorny wood,<br>That rends the thorns and is rent with the thorns,<br>Seeking a way and straying from the way,<br>Not knowing how to find the open air,<br>But toiling desperately to find it out—<br>Torment myself to catch the English [[motifs/crown\|crown]].<br>And from that torment I will free myself,<br>Or hew my way out with a bloody axe.<br>Why, I can smile, and murder whiles I smile,<br>And cry ‘Content!’ to that which grieves my heart,<br>And wet my cheeks with artificial [[motifs/tears\|tears]],<br>And frame my face to all occasions.<br>I’ll drown more sailors than the [[characters/mermaid\|mermaid]] shall;<br>I’ll slay more gazers than the basilisk;<br>I’ll play the orator as [[motifs/well\|well]] as Nestor,<br>Deceive more slyly than [[characters/ulysses\|Ulysses]] could,<br>And, like a Sinon, take another Troy.<br>I can add colours to the chameleon,<br>Change shapes with [[characters/proteus\|Proteus]] for advantages,<br>And set the murderous Machiavel to school.<br>Can I do this, and cannot get a [[motifs/crown\|crown]]?<br>Tut, were it farther off, I’ll pluck it down. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | 3.3 Two chairs of state. Flourish. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis of France, his sister the Lady Bona, Lord Bourbon his admiral, Prince Edward, [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]], and the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]. Louis goes up upon the state, sits, and riseth up again |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | Fair [[characters/queen\|Queen]] of England, worthy Margaret,<br>Sit down with us. It ill befits thy state<br>And birth that thou shouldst stand while Louis<br>doth sit. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | No, mighty [[characters/king-of-france\|King of France]], now Margaret<br>Must strike her sail and learn a while to serve<br>Where kings command. I was, I must confess,<br>Great Albion’s [[characters/queen\|queen]] in former golden days,<br>But now mischance hath trod my title down,<br>And with dishonour laid me on the ground,<br>Where I must take like seat unto my fortune<br>And to my humble state conform myself. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | Why, say, fair [[characters/queen\|Queen]], whence springs this deep [[concepts/despair\|despair]] ? |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | From such a cause as fills mine eyes with [[motifs/tears\|tears]]<br>And stops my tongue, while heart is drowned in cares. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | Whate’er it be, be thou still like thyself,<br>And sit thee by our side.<br>Seats her by him<br>Yield not thy neck<br>To fortune’s yoke, but let thy dauntless mind<br>Still ride in triumph over all mischance.<br>Be plain, [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]], and tell thy grief.<br>It shall be eased if France can yield relief. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Those gracious words revive my drooping thoughts,<br>And give my tongue-tied sorrows leave to speak.<br>Now, therefore, be it known to noble Louis<br>That Henry, sole possessor of my [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>Is of a [[archetypes/king\|king]] become a banished man,<br>And forced to live in Scotland a forlorn,<br>While proud ambitious Edward, [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]],<br>Usurps the regal title and the seat<br>Of England’s true-anointed lawful [[archetypes/king\|King]].<br>This is the cause that I, poor Margaret,<br>With this my son, Prince Edward, Henry’s heir,<br>Am come to crave thy just and lawful aid.<br>An if thou fail us all our [[concepts/hope\|hope]] is done.<br>Scotland hath will to help, but cannot help;<br>Our people and our peers are both misled,<br>Our treasure seized, our soldiers put to flight,<br>And, as thou seest, ourselves in heavy plight. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | Renowned [[characters/queen\|Queen]], with patience calm the [[motifs/storm\|storm]],<br>While we bethink a means to break it off. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | The more we stay, the stronger grows our foe. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | The more I stay, the more I’ll succour thee. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | O, but impatience waiteth on true sorrow. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]] |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | And see where comes the breeder of my sorrow. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | What’s he approacheth boldly to our presence? |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Our [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]], Edward’s greatest friend. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | Welcome, brave Warwick. What brings thee to France ?<br>He descends. She ariseth |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | (aside)<br>Ay, now begins a second [[motifs/storm\|storm]] to rise,<br>For this is he that moves both [[motifs/wind\|wind]] and tide. |
| WARWICK | (to [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis)<br>From worthy Edward, [[archetypes/king\|King]] of Albion,<br>My lord and sovereign, and thy vowed friend,<br>I come in kindness and unfeigned [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>First, to do greetings to thy royal person,<br>And then, to crave a league of amity,<br>And lastly, to confirm that amity<br>With nuptial knot, if thou vouchsafe to grant<br>That virtuous Lady Bona, thy fair sister,<br>To England’s [[archetypes/king\|King]] in lawful [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]]. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | (aside)<br>If that go forward, Henry’s [[concepts/hope\|hope]] is done. |
| WARWICK | (to Lady Bona)<br>And, gracious madam, in our [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s behalf<br>I am commanded, with your leave and favour,<br>Humbly to kiss your hand, and with my tongue<br>To tell the passion of my sovereign’s heart,<br>Where fame, late ent‘ring at his heedful ears,<br>Hath placed thy [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]]’s image and thy virtue. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis and Lady Bona, hear me speak<br>Before you answer Warwick. His demand<br>Springs not from Edward’s [[motifs/well\|well]]-meant honest [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>But from deceit, bred by necessity.<br>For how can tyrants safely govern [[motifs/home\|home]]<br>Unless abroad they purchase great alliance ?<br>To prove him [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]] this reason may suffice—<br>That Henry liveth still; but were he dead,<br>Yet here Prince Edward stands, [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry’s son.<br>Look, therefore, Louis, that by this league and<br>[[motifs/marriage\|marriage]]<br>Thou draw not on thy danger and dishonour,<br>For though usurpers sway the rule a while,<br>Yet heav’ns are just and [[motifs/time\|time]] suppresseth wrongs. |
| WARWICK | Injurious Margaret. |
| PRINCE EDWARD | And why not ‘[[characters/queen\|Queen]]’? |
| WARWICK | Because thy father Henry did usurp,<br>And thou no more art prince than she is [[characters/queen\|queen]]. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | Then Warwick disannuls great [[characters/john-of-gaunt\|John of Gaunt]],<br>Which did subdue the greatest part of Spain;<br>And, after [[characters/john-of-gaunt\|John of Gaunt]], Henry the Fourth,<br>Whose wisdom was a [[motifs/mirror\|mirror]] to the wisest;<br>And, after that wise prince, Henry the Fifth,<br>Who by his prowess conquered all France.<br>From these our Henry lineally descends. |
| WARWICK | [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], how haps it in this smooth discourse<br>You told not how Henry the Sixth hath lost<br>All that which Henry the Fifth had gotten ?<br>Methinks these peers of France should smile at that.<br>But for the rest, you tell a pedigree<br>Of threescore and two years—a silly [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>To make prescription for a kingdom’s worth. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | Why, Warwick, canst thou speak against thy liege,<br>Whom thou obeyedest thirty and six years,<br>And not bewray thy treason with a blush? |
| WARWICK | Can [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], that did ever fence the right,<br>Now buckler falsehood with a pedigree?<br>For shame—leave Henry, and call Edward [[archetypes/king\|king]]. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | Call him my [[archetypes/king\|king]] by whose injurious doom<br>My elder brother, the Lord Aubrey Vere,<br>Was done to [[motifs/death\|death]] ? And more than so, my father,<br>Even in the downfall of his mellowed years,<br>When [[concepts/nature\|nature]] brought him to the door of [[motifs/death\|death]]?<br>No, Warwick, no—while life upholds this arm,<br>This arm upholds the house of Lancaster. |
| WARWICK | And I the house of York. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]], Prince Edward, and [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]],<br>Vouchsafe, at our request, to stand aside<br>While I use further conference with Warwick. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]] comes down from the state and, with Prince Edward and [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], stands apart |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Heavens grant that Warwick’s words bewitch him not. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | Now, Warwick, tell me even upon thy [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]],<br>Is Edward your true [[archetypes/king\|king]]? For I were loath<br>To link with him that were not lawful chosen. |
| WARWICK | Thereon I pawn my credit and mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | But is he gracious in the people’s eye? |
| WARWICK | The more that Henry was unfortunate. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | Then further, all dissembling set aside,<br>Tell me for [[concepts/truth\|truth]] the measure of his [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>Unto our sister Bona. |
| WARWICK | Such it seems<br>As may beseem a monarch like himself.<br>Myself have often heard him say and swear<br>That this his [[motifs/love\|love]] was an eternal plant,<br>Whereof the root was fixed in virtue’s ground,<br>The [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]] and fruit maintained with [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]]’s sun,<br>Exempt from envy, but not from disdain,<br>Unless the Lady Bona quit his [[motifs/pain\|pain]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | (to Lady Boa)<br>Now, sister, let us hear your firm resolve. |
| LADY BONA | Your grant, or your denial, shall be mine.<br>(To Warwick) Yet I confess that often ere this day,<br>When I have heard your [[archetypes/king\|king]]’s [[motifs/desert\|desert]] recounted,<br>Mine ear hath tempted judgement to [[motifs/desire\|desire]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | (to Warwick)<br>Then, Warwick, thus—our sister shall be Edward’s.<br>And now, forthwith, shall articles be drawn<br>Touching the jointure that your [[archetypes/king\|king]] must make,<br>Which with her dowry shall be counterpoised.<br>(To [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]]) Draw near, [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]], and<br>be a witness<br>That Bona shall be wife to the English [[archetypes/king\|king]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]], Prince Edward, and [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] come forward |
| PRINCE EDWARD | To Edward, but not to the English [[archetypes/king\|king]]. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Deceitful Warwick—it was thy device<br>By this alliance to make void my suit!<br>Before thy coming Louis was Henry’s friend. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | And still is friend to him and Margaret.<br>But if your title to the [[motifs/crown\|crown]] be weak,<br>As may appear by Edward’s good success,<br>Then ’tis but reason that I be released<br>From giving aid which late I promised.<br>Yet shall you have all kindness at my hand<br>That your estate requires and mine can yield. |
| WARWICK | (to [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]])<br>Henry now lives in Scotland at his ease,<br>Where having nothing, nothing can he lose.<br>And as for you yourself, our quondam [[characters/queen\|queen]],<br>You have a father able to maintain you,<br>And better ’twere you troubled him than France. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Peace, impudent and shameless Warwick, peace!<br>Proud setter-up and puller-down of kings!<br>I will not hence till, with my talk and [[motifs/tears\|tears]],<br>Both full of [[concepts/truth\|truth]], I make [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis behold<br>Thy sly conveyance and thy lord’s false [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>Post blowing a horn within<br><br>For both of you are [[motifs/birds\|birds]] of selfsame feather. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | Warwick, this is some post to us or thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Post |
| POST | (to Warwick)<br>My lord ambassador, these letters are for you,<br>Sent from your brother Marquis Montague;<br>(To Louis) These from our [[archetypes/king\|King]] unto your majesty;<br>(To [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]])<br>And, madam, these for you, from whom I know not.<br>They all read their letters |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | (to Prince Edward)<br>I like it [[motifs/well\|well]] that our fair [[characters/queen\|Queen]] and mistress<br>Smiles at her news, while Warwick frowns at his. |
| PRINCE EDWARD | Nay, mark how Louis stamps as he were nettled.<br>I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] all’s for the best. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | Warwick, what are thy news ? And yours, fair [[characters/queen\|Queen]]? |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Mine, such as fill my heart with unhoped joys. |
| WARWICK | Mine, full of sorrow and heart’s discontent. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | What! Has your [[archetypes/king\|king]] married the Lady Gray?<br>And now to soothe your forgery and his,<br>Sends me a paper to persuade me patience?<br>Is this th’alliance that he seeks with France?<br>Dare he presume to scorn us in this manner? |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | I told your majesty as much before—<br>This proveth Edward’s [[motifs/love\|love]] and Warwick’s honesty. |
| WARWICK | [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis, I here protest in sight of heaven<br>And by the [[concepts/hope\|hope]] I have of heavenly bliss,<br>That I am clear from this misdeed of Edward‘s,<br>No more my [[archetypes/king\|king]], for he dishonours me,<br>But most himself, if he could see his shame.<br>Did I forget that by the house of York<br>My father came untimely to his [[motifs/death\|death]]?<br>Did I let pass th’abuse done to my niece?<br>Did I impale him with the regal [[motifs/crown\|crown]]?<br>Did I put Henry from his native right?<br>And am I guerdoned at the last with shame?<br>Shame on himself, for my [[motifs/desert\|desert]] is [[concepts/honour\|honour]].<br>And to repair my [[concepts/honour\|honour]], lost for him,<br>I here renounce him and return to Henry.<br>(To [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]]) My noble [[characters/queen\|Queen]], let former<br>grudges pass,<br>And henceforth I am thy true servitor.<br>I will [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]] his wrong to Lady Bona<br>And replant Henry in his former state. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Warwick, these words have turned my hate to [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>And I forgive and quite forget old faults,<br>And joy that thou becom’st [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry’s friend. |
| WARWICK | So much his friend, ay, his unfeigned friend,<br>That if [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis vouchsafe to furnish us<br>With some few bands of chosen soldiers,<br>I’ll undertake to land them on our coast<br>And force the [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]] from his seat by war.<br>’Tis not his new-made bride shall succour him.<br>And as for [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], as my letters tell me,<br>He’s very likely now to fall from him<br>For matching more for wanton lust than [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>Or than for strength and safety of our country. |
| LADY BONA | (to [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis)<br>Dear brother, how shall Bona be revenged,<br>But by thy help to this distressed [[characters/queen\|Queen]]? |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | (to [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis)<br>Renowned Prince, how shall poor Henry live<br>Unless thou rescue him from foul [[concepts/despair\|despair]]? |
| LADY BONA | (to [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis)<br>My quarrel and this English [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s are one. |
| WARWICK | And mine, fair Lady Bona, joins with yours. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | And mine with hers, and thine, and Margaret’s.<br>Therefore at last I firmly am resolved:<br>You shall have aid. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Let me give humble thanks for all at once. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | (to the Post)<br>Then, England’s messenger, return in post<br>And tell false Edward, thy supposed [[archetypes/king\|king]],<br>That Louis of France is sending over masquers<br>To revel it with him and his new bride.<br>Thou seest what’s passed, go [[concepts/fear\|fear]] thy [[archetypes/king\|king]] withal. |
| LADY BONA | (to the Post)<br>Tell him, in [[concepts/hope\|hope]] he’ll prove a widower shortly,<br>I’ll wear the willow garland for his sake. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | (to the Post)<br>Tell him my mourning weeds are laid aside,<br>And I am ready to put armour on. |
| WARWICK | (to the Post)<br>Tell him from me that he hath done me wrong,<br>And therefore I’ll uncrown him ere’t be long.<br>(Giving money) There’s thy reward—be gone. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Post |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | But, Warwick, thou and [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], with five thousand<br>men,<br>Shall [[motifs/cross\|cross]] the seas and bid false Edward battle;<br>And, as occasion serves, this noble [[characters/queen\|Queen]]<br>And Prince shall follow with a fresh supply.<br>Yet, ere thou go, but answer me one [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]:<br>What pledge have we of thy firm loyalty? |
| WARWICK | This shall assure my constant loyalty :<br>That if our [[characters/queen\|Queen]] and this young Prince agree,<br>I’ll join mine eldest daughter and my joy<br>To him forthwith in holy wedlock bands. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Yes, I agree, and thank you for your motion.<br>(To Prince Edward) Son Edward, she is fair and virtuous,<br>Therefore delay not. Give thy hand to Warwick,<br>And with thy hand thy [[concepts/faith\|faith]] irrevocable<br>That only Warwick’s daughter shall be thine. |
| PRINCE EDWARD | Yes, I accept her, for she [[motifs/well\|well]] deserves it,<br>And here to pledge my vow I give my hand.<br>He and Warwick clasp hands |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] LOUIS | Why stay we now? These soldiers shall be levied,<br>And thou, Lord Bourbon, our high admiral,<br>Shall waft them over with our royal fleet.<br>I long till Edward fall by war’s mischance<br>For mocking [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] with a dame of France. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but Warwick |
| WARWICK | I came from Edward as ambassador,<br>But I return his sworn and mortal foe.<br>Matter of [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] was the charge he gave me,<br>But dreadful war shall answer his demand.<br>Had he none else to make a stale but me?<br>Then none but I shall turn his jest to sorrow.<br>I was the chief that raised him to the [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>And I’ll be chief to bring him down again.<br>Not that I pity Henry’s misery,<br>But seek [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]] on Edward’s mockery. Exit |

### Atto 4, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], the Duke of Somerset, and the Marquis of Montague |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now tell me, brother [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], what think you<br>Of this new [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] with the Lady Gray?<br>Hath not our brother made a worthy choice ? |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Alas, you know ’tis far from hence to France;<br>How could he stay till Warwick made return? |
| SOMERSET | My lords, forbear this talk—here comes the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward, the Lady Gray his |
| SOMERSET | [[characters/queen\|Queen]], the Earl of Pembroke, and the Lords |
| *(didascalia)* | Stafford and [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]]. Four stand on one side of |
| *(didascalia)* | the [[archetypes/king\|King]], and four on the other |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And his [[motifs/well\|well]]-chosen bride. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | I mind to tell him plainly what I think. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Now, brother of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], how like you our choice,<br>That you stand pensive, as half-malcontent? |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | As [[motifs/well\|well]] as Louis of France, or the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]],<br>Which are so weak of [[concepts/courage\|courage]] and in judgement<br>That they’ll take no offence at our abuse. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Suppose they take offence without a cause—<br>They are but Louis and Warwick; I am Edward,<br>Your [[archetypes/king\|king]] and Warwick’s, and must have my will. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And you shall have your will, because our [[archetypes/king\|king]].<br>Yet hasty [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] seldom proveth [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Yea, brother [[characters/richard\|Richard]], are you offended too ? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Not I, no—God forbid that I should wish them severed<br>Whom God hath joined together. Ay, and ’twere pity<br>To sunder them that yoke so [[motifs/well\|well]] together. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Setting your scorns and your mislike aside,<br>Tell me some reason why the Lady Gray<br>Should not become my wife and England’s [[characters/queen\|queen]].<br>And you too, Somerset and Montague,<br>Speak freely what you think. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Then this is my opinion: that [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis<br>Becomes your enemy for mocking him<br>About the [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] of the Lady Bona. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And Warwick, doing what you gave in charge,<br>Is now dishonoured by this new [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | What if both Louis and Warwick be appeased<br>By such invention as I can devise? |
| MONTAGUE | Yet, to have joined with France in such alliance<br>Would more have strengthened this our<br>commonwealth<br>’Gainst foreign storms than any [[motifs/home\|home]]-bred [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]]. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Why, knows not Montague that of itself<br>England is safe, if true within itself? |
| MONTAGUE | But the safer when ’tis backed with France. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | ’Tis better using France than trusting France.<br>Let us be backed with God and with the seas<br>Which he hath giv’n for fence impregnable,<br>And with their helps only defend ourselves.<br>In them and in ourselves our safety lies. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | For this one speech Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] [[motifs/well\|well]] deserves<br>To have the heir of the Lord Hungerford. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Ay, what of that? It was my will and grant—<br>And for this once my will shall stand for law. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And yet, methinks, your grace hath not done [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>To give the heir and daughter of Lord Scales<br>Unto the brother of your loving bride.<br>She better would have fitted me or [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]],<br>But in your bride you bury brotherhood. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Or else you would not have bestowed the heir<br>Of the Lord Bonville on your new wife’s son,<br>And leave your brothers to go [[characters/speed\|speed]] elsewhere. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Alas, poor [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], is it for a wife<br>That thou art malcontent? I will provide thee. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | In choosing for yourself you showed your judgement,<br>Which being shallow, you shall give me leave<br>To play the broker in mine own behalf,<br>And to that end I shortly mind to leave you. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Leave me, or tarry. Edward will be [[archetypes/king\|king]],<br>And not be tied unto his brother’s will. |
| LADY GRAY | My lords, before it pleased his majesty<br>To raise my state to title of a [[characters/queen\|queen]],<br>Do me but right, and you must all confess<br>That I was not ignoble of descent—<br>And meaner than myself have had like fortune.<br>But as this title honours me and mine,<br>So your dislikes, to whom I would be pleasing,<br>Doth cloud my joys with danger and with sorrow. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | My [[motifs/love\|love]], forbear to fawn upon their frowns.<br>What danger or what sorrow can befall thee<br>So long as Edward is thy constant friend,<br>And their true sovereign, whom they must obey?<br>Nay, whom they shall obey, and [[motifs/love\|love]] thee too—<br>Unless they seek for hatred at my hands,<br>Which if they do, yet will I keep thee safe,<br>And they shall feel the vengeance of my wrath. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (aside)<br>I hear, yet say not much, but think the more. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Post from France |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Now, messenger, what letters or what news from France ? |
| POST | My sovereign liege, no letters and few words,<br>But such as I, without your special pardon,<br>Dare not relate. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Go to, we pardon thee. Therefore, in brief,<br>Tell me their words as near as thou canst guess them.<br>What answer makes [[archetypes/king\|King]] Louis unto our letters? |
| POST | At my depart these were his very words:<br>‘Go tell false Edward, thy supposed [[archetypes/king\|king]],<br>That Louis of France is sending over masquers<br>To revel it with him and his new bride.’ |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Is Louis so brave ? Belike he thinks me Henry.<br>But what said Lady Bona to my [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]]? |
| POST | These were her words, uttered with mild disdain:<br>‘Tell him in [[concepts/hope\|hope]] he’ll prove a widower shortly,<br>I’ll wear the willow garland for his sake.’ |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | ame not her, she could say little less;<br>She had the wrong. But what said Henry’s [[characters/queen\|queen]]?<br>For I have heard that she was there in place. |
| POST | ‘Tell him‘, quoth she, ’my mourning weeds are done,<br>And I am ready to put armour on.’ |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Belike she minds to play the Amazon.<br>But what said Warwick to these injuries? |
| POST | He, more incensed against your majesty<br>Than all the rest, discharged me with these words:<br>‘Tell him from me that he hath done me wrong,<br>And therefore I’ll uncrown him ere’t be long.’ |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Ha! Durst the traitor breathe out so proud words?<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]], I will arm me, being thus forewarned.<br>They shall have wars and pay for their presumption.<br>But say, is Warwick friends with Margaret? |
| POST | Ay, gracious sovereign, they are so linked in [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]]<br>That young Prince Edward marries Warwick’s daughter. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Belike the elder; [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] will have the younger.<br>Now, brother [[archetypes/king\|King]], farewell, and sit you fast,<br>For I will hence to Warwick’s other daughter,<br>That, though I want a kingdom, yet in [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]]<br>I may not prove inferior to yourself.<br>You that [[motifs/love\|love]] me and Warwick, follow me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], and Somerset follows |
| *(didascalia)* | Not I—aside my thoughts aim at a further matter. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I stay not for the [[motifs/love\|love]] of Edward, but the [[motifs/crown\|crown]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] and Somerset both gone to Warwick?<br>Yet am I armed against the worst can happen,<br>And haste is needful in this desp’rate case.<br>Pembroke and Stafford, you in our behalf<br>Go levy men and make prepare for war.<br>They are already, or quickly will be, landed.<br>Myself in person will straight follow you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Pembroke and Stafford |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | But ere I go, [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] and Montague,<br>Resolve my [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]. You twain, of all the rest,<br>Are near’st to Warwick by [[motifs/blood\|blood]] and by alliance.<br>Tell me if you [[motifs/love\|love]] Warwick more than me.<br>If it be so, then both depart to him—<br>I rather wish you foes than hollow friends.<br>But if you mind to hold your true obedience,<br>Give me assurance with some friendly vow<br>That I may never have you in suspect. |
| MONTAGUE | So God help Montague as he proves true. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | And [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] as he favours Edward’s cause. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Now, brother [[characters/richard\|Richard]], will you stand by us? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, in despite of all that shall withstand you. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Why, so. Then am I sure of victory.<br>Now, therefore, let us hence and lose no hour<br>Till we meet Warwick with his foreign power. Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earls of Warwick and [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] in England, with French soldiers |
| WARWICK | Trust me, my lord, all hitherto goes [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>The common sort by numbers swarm to us. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Dukes of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] and Somerset |
| WARWICK | But see where Somerset and [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] comes.<br>Speak suddenly, my lords, are we all friends ? |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | [[concepts/fear\|Fear]] not that, my lord. |
| WARWICK | Then, gentle [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], welcome unto Warwick—<br>And welcome, Somerset. I hold it cowardice<br>To rest mistrustful where a noble heart<br>Hath pawned an open hand in sign of [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>Else might I think that [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], Edward’s brother,<br>Were but a feigned friend to our proceedings.<br>But come, sweet [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], my daughter shall be thine.<br>And now what rests but, in [[motifs/night\|night]]’s coverture,<br>Thy brother being carelessly encamped,<br>His soldiers lurking in the towns about,<br>And but attended by a simple guard,<br>We may surprise and take him at our pleasure?<br>Our scouts have found the adventure very easy;<br>That, as [[characters/ulysses\|Ulysses]] and stout Diomed<br>With sleight and manhood stole to Rhesus’ tents<br>And brought from thence the Thracian fatal steeds,<br>So we, [[motifs/well\|well]] covered with the [[motifs/night\|night]]’s black mantle,<br>At unawares may beat down Edward’s guard<br>And seize himself—I say not ‘slaughter him’,<br>For I intend but only to surprise him.<br>You that will follow me to this attempt,<br>Applaud the name of Henry with your leader.<br>They all cry ‘Henry’<br>Why, then, let’s on our way in silent sort,<br>For Warwick and his friends, God and Saint George! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter three Watchmen, to guard [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward’s tent |
| FIRST WATCHMAN | Come on, my masters, each man take his stand.<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] by this is set him down to [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]. |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | What, will he not to bed? |
| FIRST WATCHMAN | Why, no—for he hath made a solemn vow<br>Never to lie and take his natural rest<br>Till Warwick or himself be quite suppressed. |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | Tomorrow then belike shall be the day,<br>If Warwick be so near as men report. |
| THIRD WATCHMAN | But say, I pray, what nobleman is that<br>That with the [[archetypes/king\|King]] here resteth in his tent? |
| FIRST WATCHMAN | ’Tis the Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s chiefest friend. |
| THIRD WATCHMAN | O, is it so ? But why commands the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>That his chief followers lodge in towns about him,<br>While he himself keeps in the cold field? |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | ’Tis the more [[concepts/honour\|honour]], because more dangerous. |
| THIRD WATCHMAN | Ay, but give me worship and quietness—<br>I like it better than a dangerous [[concepts/honour\|honour]].<br>If Warwick knew in what estate he stands,<br>’Tis to be doubted he would waken him. |
| FIRST WATCHMAN | Unless our halberds did shut up his passage. |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | Ay, wherefore else guard we his royal tent<br>But to defend his person from [[motifs/night\|night]]-foes? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter silently the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]], George Duke of |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], and the Duke of<br><br>Somerset, with French soldiers |
| WARWICK | This is his tent—and see where stand his guard.<br>[[concepts/courage\|Courage]], my masters—[[concepts/honour\|honour]] now or never!<br>But follow me, and Edward shall be ours. |
| FIRST WATCHMAN | Who goes there? |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | Stay or thou diest.<br>Warwick and the rest all cry ‘Warwick, Warwick!’<br><br>and set upon the guard, who fly, crying ‘Arm, arm!’<br><br>Warwick and the rest follow them |

### Atto 4, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | With the drummer playing and trumpeter sounding, enter the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]], the Duke of Somerset, and the rest bringing [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward out in his gown, sitting in a chair. [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]] and Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] flies over the stage |
| SOMERSET | What are they that fly there? |
| WARWICK | [[characters/richard\|Richard]] and [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]]—let them go. Here is the Duke. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | ‘The Duke’! Why, Warwick, when we parted,<br>Thou calledst me [[archetypes/king\|king]]. |
| WARWICK | Ay, but the case is altered.<br>When you disgraced me in my embassade,<br>Then I degraded you from being [[archetypes/king\|king]],<br>And come now to create you [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]].<br>Alas, how should you govern any kingdom<br>That know not how to use ambassadors,<br>Nor how to be contented with one wife,<br>Nor how to use your brothers brotherly,<br>Nor how to study for the people’s welfare,<br>Nor how to shroud yourself from enemies? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (seeing George)<br>Yea, brother of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], art thou here too?<br>Nay, then, I see that Edward needs must down.<br>Yet, Warwick, in despite of all mischance,<br>Of thee thyself and all thy complices,<br>Edward will always bear himself as [[archetypes/king\|king]].<br>Though fortune’s malice overthrow my state,<br>My mind exceeds the compass of her [[motifs/wheel\|wheel]]. |
| WARWICK | Then, for his mind, be Edward England’s [[archetypes/king\|king]].<br>Warwick takes off Edward’s [[motifs/crown\|crown]]<br>But Henry now shall wear the English [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>And be true [[archetypes/king\|king]] indeed, thou but the [[archetypes/shadow\|shadow]].<br>My lord of Somerset, at my request,<br>See that, forthwith, Duke Edward be conveyed<br>Unto my brother, Archbishop of York.<br>When I have fought with Pembroke and his fellows,<br>I’ll follow you, and tell what answer<br>Louis and the Lady Bona send to him.<br>Now for a while farewell, good [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]].<br>They begin to lead Edward out forcibly |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | What fates impose, that men must needs abide.<br>It boots not to resist both [[motifs/wind\|wind]] and tide. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some with Edward |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | What now remains, my lords, for us to do<br>But march to [[settings/london\|London]] with our soldiers? |
| WARWICK | Ay, that’s the first thing that we have to do—<br>To free [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry from [[concepts/imprisonment\|imprisonment]]<br>And see him seated in the regal throne. Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Earl Rivers and his sister, Lady Gray, Edward’s [[characters/queen\|queen]] |
| RIVERS | Madam, what makes you in this sudden change? |
| LADY GRAY | Why, brother Rivers, are you yet to learn<br>What late misfortune is befall’n [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward? |
| RIVERS | What? Loss of some pitched battle against Warwick? |
| LADY GRAY | No, but the loss of his own royal person. |
| RIVERS | Then is my sovereign slain? |
| LADY GRAY | Ay, almost stain—for he is taken prisoner,<br>Either betrayed by falsehood of his guard<br>Or by his foe surprised at unawares,<br>And, as I further have to understand,<br>Is new committed to the Bishop of York,<br>Fell Warwick’s brother, and by that our foe. |
| RIVERS | These news, I must confess, are full of grief.<br>Yet, gracious madam, bear it as you may.<br>Warwick may lose, that now hath won the day. |
| LADY GRAY | Till then fair [[concepts/hope\|hope]] must hinder life’s [[motifs/decay\|decay]],<br>And I the rather wean me from [[concepts/despair\|despair]]<br>For [[motifs/love\|love]] of Edward’s offspring in my womb.<br>This is it that makes me bridle passion<br>And bear with mildness my misfortune’s [[motifs/cross\|cross]].<br>Ay, ay, for this I draw in many a tear<br>And stop the rising of [[motifs/blood\|blood]]-sucking sighs,<br>Lest with my sighs or [[motifs/tears\|tears]] I blast or drown<br>[[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward’s fruit, true heir to th’English [[motifs/crown\|crown]]. |
| RIVERS | But, madam, where is Warwick then become? |
| LADY GRAY | I am informed that he comes towards [[settings/london\|London]]<br>To set the [[motifs/crown\|crown]] once more on Henry’s head.<br>Guess thou the rest—[[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward’s friends must down.<br>But to prevent the [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]]’s violence—<br>For trust not him that hath once broken [[concepts/faith\|faith]]—<br>I’ll hence forthwith unto the sanctuary,<br>To save at least the heir of Edward’s right.<br>There shall I rest secure from force and fraud.<br>Come, therefore, let us fly while we may fly.<br>If Warwick take us, we are sure to die. Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 6

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], and Sir William Stanley, with soldiers |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now my lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] and Sir William Stanley,<br>Leave off to [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] why I drew you hither<br>Into this chiefest thicket of the park.<br>Thus stands the case: you know our [[archetypes/king\|King]], my brother,<br>Is prisoner to the Bishop here, at whose hands<br>He hath good usage and great liberty,<br>And, often but attended with weak guard,<br>Comes hunting this way to disport himself.<br>I have advertised him by secret means<br>That if about this hour he make this way<br>Under the colour of his usual game,<br>He shall here find his friends with [[motifs/horse\|horse]] and men<br>To set him free from his captivity. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward and a Huntsman with him |
| HUNTSMAN | This way, my lord—for this way lies the game. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Nay, this way, man—see where the huntsmen stand.<br>Now, brother of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]], Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], and the<br>rest,<br>Stand you thus close to steal the Bishop’s deer? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Brother, the [[motifs/time\|time]] and case requireth haste.<br>Your [[motifs/horse\|horse]] stands ready at the park corner. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | But whither shall we then? |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | To Lynn, my lord,<br>And shipped from thence to Flanders. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] aside |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]] guessed, believe me—for that was my meaning. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Stanley, I will requite thy forwardness. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | But wherefore stay we? ’Tis no [[motifs/time\|time]] to talk. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Huntsman, what sayst thou? Wilt thou go along? |
| HUNTSMAN | Better do so than tarry and be hanged. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Come then, away—let’s have no more ado. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Bishop, farewell—shield thee from Warwick’s frown,<br>And pray that I may repossess the [[motifs/crown\|crown]]. Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 7

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]] and George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] with the [[motifs/crown\|crown]]. Then enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry, the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], the Duke of Somerset withl young Henry Ear of Richmond, the Marquis of Montague, and the Lieutenant of the Tower |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Master Lieutenant, now that God and friends<br>Have shaken Edward from the regal seat<br>And turned my captive state to liberty,<br>My [[concepts/fear\|fear]] to [[concepts/hope\|hope]], my sorrows unto joys,<br>At our enlargement what are thy due fees? |
| LIEUTENANT | Subjects may challenge nothing of their sovereigns—<br>But if an humble [[motifs/prayer\|prayer]] may prevail,<br>I then crave pardon of your majesty. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | For what, Lieutenant? For [[motifs/well\|well]] using me?<br>Nay, be thou sure I’ll [[motifs/well\|well]] requite thy kindness,<br>For that it made my prisonment a pleasure—<br>Ay, such a pleasure as encagèd [[motifs/birds\|birds]]<br>Conceive when, after many moody thoughts,<br>At last by notes of household harmony<br>They quite forget their loss of liberty.<br>But, Warwick, after God, thou sett’st me free,<br>And chiefly therefore I thank God and thee.<br>He was the author, thou the instrument.<br>Therefore, that I may conquer fortune’s spite<br>By living low, where fortune cannot hurt me,<br>And that the people of this blessed land<br>May not be punished with my thwarting [[motifs/stars\|stars]],<br>Warwick, although my head still wear the [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>I here resign my government to thee,<br>For thou art fortunate in all thy deeds. |
| WARWICK | Your grace hath still been famed for virtuous,<br>And now may seem as wise as virtuous<br>By spying and avoiding fortune’s malice,<br>For few men rightly temper with the [[motifs/stars\|stars]].<br>Yet in this one thing let me blame your grace:<br>For choosing me when [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] is in place. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | No, Warwick, thou art worthy of the sway,<br>To whom the heav’ns in thy nativity<br>Adjudged an olive branch and laurel [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>As likely to be blest in peace and war.<br>And therefore I yield thee my free consent. |
| WARWICK | And I choose [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] only for Protector. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Warwick and [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], give me both your hands.<br>Now join your hands, and with your hands your<br>hearts,<br>That no dissension hinder government.<br>I make you both Protectors of this land,<br>While I myself will lead a private life<br>And in devotion spend my latter days,<br>To sin’s rebuke and my creator’s praise. |
| WARWICK | What answers [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] to his sovereign’s will? |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | That he consents, if Warwick yield consent,<br>For on thy fortune I repose myself. |
| WARWICK | Why, then, though loath, yet must I be content.<br>We’ll yoke together, like a [[topoi/double\|double]] [[archetypes/shadow\|shadow]]<br>To Henry’s [[concepts/body\|body]], and supply his place—<br>I mean in bearing weight of government—<br>While he enjoys the [[concepts/honour\|honour]] and his ease.<br>And, [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], now then it is more than needful<br>Forthwith that Edward be pronounced a traitor,<br>And all his lands and goods be confiscate. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | What else? And that succession be determined. |
| WARWICK | Ay, therein [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] shall not want his part. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | But with the first of all your chief affairs,<br>Let me entreat—for I command no more—<br>That Margaret your [[characters/queen\|queen]] and my son Edward<br>Be sent for, to return from France with [[characters/speed\|speed]].<br>For, till I see them here, by doubtful [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>My joy of liberty is half eclipsed. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | It shall be done, my sovereign, with all [[characters/speed\|speed]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | My lord of Somerset, what youth is that<br>Of whom you seem to have so tender care? |
| SOMERSET | My liege, it is young Henry, [[characters/earl-of-richmond\|Earl of Richmond]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Come hither, England’s [[concepts/hope\|hope]].<br>[[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry lays his hand on Richmond’s head If secret powers<br>Suggest but [[concepts/truth\|truth]] to my divining thoughts,<br>This pretty lad will prove our country’s bliss.<br>His looks are full of peaceful majesty,<br>His head by [[concepts/nature\|nature]] framed to wear a [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>His hand to wield a sceptre, and himself<br>Likely in [[motifs/time\|time]] to bless a regal throne.<br>Make much of him, my lords, for this is he<br>Must help you more than you are hurt by me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Post |
| WARWICK | What news, my friend? |
| POST | That Edward is escaped from your brother<br>And fled, as he hears since, to Burgundy. |
| WARWICK | Unsavoury news—but how made he escape ? |
| POST | He was conveyed by [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]]<br>And the Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], who attended him<br>In secret ambush on the forest side<br>And from the Bishop’s huntsmen rescued him—<br>For hunting was his daily exercise. |
| WARWICK | My brother was too careless of his charge.<br>(To [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry) But let us hence, my sovereign, to<br>provide<br>A salve for any sore that may betide. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but Somerset, Richmond, and [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] |
| SOMERSET | (to [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]])<br>My lord, I like not of this flight of Edward’s,<br>For doubtless Burgundy will yield him help,<br>And we shall have more wars before’t be long.<br>As Henry’s late presaging prophecy<br>Did glad my heart with [[concepts/hope\|hope]] of this young Richmond,<br>So doth my heart misgive me, in these conflicts,<br>What may befall him, to his harm and ours.<br>Therefore, Lord [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], to prevent the worst,<br>Forthwith we’ll send him hence to Brittany,<br>Till storms be past of civil enmity. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | Ay, for if Edward repossess the [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>’Tis like that Richmond with the rest shall down. |
| SOMERSET | It shall be so—he shall to Brittany.<br>Come, therefore, let’s about it speedily. Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 8

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward, [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], and Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], With a troop of Hollanders |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Now, brother [[characters/richard\|Richard]], Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], and the rest,<br>Yet thus far fortune maketh us amends,<br>And says that once more I shall interchange<br>My waned state for Henry’s regal [[motifs/crown\|crown]].<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]] have we passed and now repassed the seas<br>And brought desired help from Burgundy.<br>What then remains, we being thus arrived<br>From Ravenspurgh haven before the gates of York,<br>But that we enter, as into our dukedom ? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] knocks at the gates of York |
