| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The gates made fast? Brother, I like not this.<br>For many men that stumble at the threshold<br>Are [[motifs/well\|well]] foretold that danger lurks within. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Tush, man, abodements must not now affright us.<br>By fair or foul means we must enter in,<br>For hither will our friends repair to us. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | My liege, I’ll knock once more to summon them.<br>He knocks. Enter, on the walls, the Mayor and aldermen of York |
| MAYOR | My lords, we were forewarned of your coming,<br>And shut the gates for safety of oursetves—<br>For now we owe allegiance unto Henry. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | But, Master Mayor, if Henry be your [[archetypes/king\|king]],<br>Yet Edward at the least is [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]]. |
| MAYOR | True, my good lord, I know you for no less. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Why, and I challenge nothing but my dukedom,<br>As being [[motifs/well\|well]] content with that alone. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (aside)<br>But when the fox hath once got in his nose,<br>He’ll soon find means to make the [[concepts/body\|body]] follow. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Why, Master Mayor, why stand you in a [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]?<br>Open the gates—we are [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry’s friends. |
| MAYOR | Ay, say you so? The gates shall then be opened.<br>They descend |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | A wise stout captain, and soon persuaded. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | The good old man would fain that all were [[motifs/well\|well]],<br>So ’twere not long of him; but being entered,<br>I [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] not, I, but we shall soon persuade<br>Both him and all his brothers unto reason. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter below the Mayor and two aldermen |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | So, Master Mayor, these gates must not be shut<br>But in the [[motifs/night\|night]] or in the [[motifs/time\|time]] of war.<br>What—[[concepts/fear\|fear]] not, man, but yield me up the keys,<br>[[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward takes some keys from the Mayor<br>For Edward will defend the town and thee,<br>And all those friends that deign to follow me.<br>March. Enter Sir John Montgomery with a drummer and soldiers |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Brother, this is Sir John Montgomery,<br>Our trusty friend, unless I be deceived. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Welcome, Sir John—but why come you in arms? |
| MONTGOMERY | To help [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward in his [[motifs/time\|time]] of [[motifs/storm\|storm]],<br>As every loyal subject ought to do. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Thanks, good Montgomery, but we now forget<br>Our title to the [[motifs/crown\|crown]], and only claim<br>Our dukedom till God please to send the rest. |
| MONTGOMERY | Then fare you [[motifs/well\|well]], for I will hence again.<br>I came to serve a [[archetypes/king\|king]] and not a duke.<br>Drummer, strike up, and let us march away. 50<br>The drummer begins to sound a march |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Nay, stay, Sir John, a while, and we’ll debate<br>By what safe means the [[motifs/crown\|crown]] may be recovered. |
| MONTGOMERY | What talk you of debating? In few words,<br>If you’ll not here proclaim yourself our [[archetypes/king\|king]]<br>I’ll leave you to your fortune and be gone<br>To keep them back that come to succour you.<br>Why shall we fight, if you pretend no title? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward)<br>Why, brother, wherefore stand you on nice points? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | When we grow stronger, then we’ll make our claim.<br>Till then ’tis wisdom to conceal our meaning. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Away with scrupulous wit! Now arms must rule. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And fearless minds climb soonest unto crowns.<br>Brother, we will proclaim you out of hand,<br>The bruit thereof will bring you many friends. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Then be it as you will, for ’tis my right,<br>And Henry but usurps the diadem. |
| MONTGOMERY | Ay, now my sovereign speaketh like himself,<br>And now will I be Edward’s champion. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound trumpet, Edward shall be here proclaimed. |
| *(didascalia)* | To Montgomery |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Come, fellow soldier, make thou proclamation. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish |
| *(didascalia)* | MONTGOMERY Edward the Fourth, by the grace of God |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | [[archetypes/king\|King]] of England and France, and Lord of Ireland—<br>And whosoe’er gainsays [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward’s right,<br>By this I challenge him to single fight.<br>He throws down his gauntlet |
| ALL | Long live Edward the Fourth! |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Thanks, brave Montgomery, and thanks unto you all.<br>If fortune serve me I’ll requite this kindness.<br>Now, for this [[motifs/night\|night]], let’s harbour here in York;<br>And when the morning sun shall raise his car<br>Above the border of this horizon,<br>We’ll forward towards Warwick and his mates.<br>For [[motifs/well\|well]] I wot that Henry is no soldier.<br>Ah, froward [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], how evil it beseems thee<br>To flatter Henry and forsake thy brother!<br>Yet, as we may, we’ll meet both thee and Warwick.<br>Come on, brave soldiers—[[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] not of the day<br>And, that once gotten, [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] not of large pay. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 9

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry, the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]], the Marquis of Montague, George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], and the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] |
| WARWICK | What counsel, lords? Edward from Belgia,<br>With hasty Germans and blunt Hollanders,<br>Hath passed in safety through the narrow seas,<br>And with his troops doth march amain to [[settings/london\|London]],<br>And many giddy people flock to him. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Let’s levy men and beat him back again. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | A little [[motifs/fire\|fire]] is quickly trodden out,<br>Which, being suffered, rivers cannot quench. |
| WARWICK | In Warwickshire I have true-hearted friends,<br>Not mutinous in peace, yet bold in war.<br>Those will I muster up. And thou, son [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]],<br>Shalt stir in Suffolk, Norfolk, and in [[characters/kent\|Kent]],<br>The knights and gentlemen to come with thee.<br>Thou, brother Montague, in [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]],<br>Northampton, and in Leicestershire shalt find<br>Men [[motifs/well\|well]] inclined to hear what thou command’st.<br>And thou, brave [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], wondrous [[motifs/well\|well]] beloved<br>In Oxfordshire, shalt muster up thy friends.<br>My sovereign, with the loving citizens,<br>Like to his island girt in with the ocean,<br>Or modest Dian circled with her nymphs,<br>Shall rest in [[settings/london\|London]] till we come to him.<br>Fair lords, take leave and stand not to reply.<br>Farewell, my sovereign. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Farewell, my [[characters/hector\|Hector]], and my Troy’s true [[concepts/hope\|hope]]. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | In sign of [[concepts/truth\|truth]], I kiss your highness’ hand.<br>He kisses [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry’s hand |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | [[motifs/well\|Well]]-minded [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], be thou fortunate. |
| MONTAGUE | Comfort, my lord, and so I take my leave. |
| *(didascalia)* | He kisses [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry’s hand |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | And thus I seal my [[concepts/truth\|truth]] and bid adieu. |
| *(didascalia)* | He kisses [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry’s hand |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Sweet [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], and my loving Montague,<br>And all at once, once more a happy farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| WARWICK | Farewell, sweet lords—let’s meet at Coventry. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally! |
| *(didascalia)* | 4.10 Erenter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry and the [[characters/duke-of-exeter\|Duke of Exeter]] |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Here at the palace will I rest a while.<br>Cousin of Exeter, what thinks your lordship?<br>Methinks the power that Edward hath in field<br>Should not be able to encounter mine. |
| EXETER | The [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] is that he will seduce the rest. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | That’s not my [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. My meed hath got me fame.<br>I have not stopped mine ears to their demands,<br>Nor posted off their suits with slow delays.<br>My pity hath been balm to heal their wounds,<br>My mildness hath allayed their swelling griefs,<br>My [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] dried their [[motifs/water\|water]]-flowing [[motifs/tears\|tears]].<br>I have not been desirous of their wealth,<br>Nor much oppressed them with great subsidies,<br>Nor forward of [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]], though they much erred.<br>Then why should they [[motifs/love\|love]] Edward more than me?<br>No, Exeter, these graces challenge grace;<br>And when the [[motifs/lion\|lion]] fawns upon the [[motifs/lamb\|lamb]],<br>The [[motifs/lamb\|lamb]] will never cease to follow him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Shout within ‘A Lancaster’, ‘A York’ |
| EXETER | Hark, hark, my ford—what shouts are these? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward and [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], with soldiers |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Seize on the shame-faced Henry—bear him hence,<br>And once again proclaim us [[archetypes/king\|King]] of England.<br>You are the fount that makes small brooks to flow.<br>Now stops thy [[motifs/spring\|spring]]—my sea shall suck them dry,<br>And swell so much the higher by their ebb.<br>Hence with him to the Tower—tet him not speak. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some with [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry and Exeter |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | And lords, towards Coventry bend we our course,<br>Where peremptory Warwick now remains.<br>The sun shines hot, and, if we use delay,<br>Cold biting [[motifs/winter\|winter]] mars our hoped-for hay. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Away betimes, before his forces join,<br>And take the great-grown traitor unawares.<br>Brave warriors, march amain towards Coventry. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]], the Mayar of Coventry, two Messengers, and others upon the walls |
| WARWICK | Where is the post that came from valiant [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | The First Messenger steps forward |
| WARWICK | How far hence is thy lord, mine honest fellow? |
| FIRST MESSENGER | By this at Dunsmore, marching hitherward. |
| WARWICK | How far off is our brother Montague?<br>Where is the post that came from Montague? |
| *(didascalia)* | the Second Messenger steps forward |
| SECOND MESSENGER | By this at Da’ntry, with a puissant troop. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Somerville to them, abovel |
| WARWICK | Say, Somerville-what says my loving son?<br>And, by thy guess, how nigh is [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] now? |
| SOMERVILLE | At Southam I did leave him with his forces,<br>And do expect him here some two hours hence.<br>A march afar off |
| WARWICK | Then [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] is at hand-I hear his drum. |
| SOMERVILLE | It is not his, my lord. Here Southam lies.<br>The drum your [[concepts/honour\|honour]] hears marcheth from Warwick. |
| WARWICK | Who should that be? Belike, unlooked-for friends. |
| SOMERVILLE | They are at hand, and you shall quickly know. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter below [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward and [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], with soldiers |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Go, trumpet, to the walls, and sound a parley. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound a parley |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | See how the surly Warwick mans the wall. |
| WARWICK | O, unbid spite—is sportful Edward come?<br>Where slept our scouts, or how are they seduced,<br>That we could hear no news of his repair? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Now, Warwick, wilt thou ope the [[motifs/city\|city]] gates,<br>Speak gentle words, and humbly bend thy knee,<br>Call Edward [[archetypes/king\|king]], and at his hands beg [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]]?<br>And he shall pardon thee these outrages. |
| WARWICK | Nay, rather, wilt thou draw thy forces hence,<br>Confess who set thee up and plucked thee down,<br>Call Warwick patron, and be penitent?<br>And thou shalt still remain the [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]]. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I thought at least he would have said ‘the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’.<br>Or did he make the jest against his will? |
| WARWICK | Is not a dukedom, sir, a goodly gift? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, by my [[concepts/faith\|faith]], for a poor earl to give.<br>I’ll do thee service for so good a gift. |
| WARWICK | ’Twas I that gave the kingdom to thy brother. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Why then, ’tis mine, if but by Warwick’s gift. |
| WARWICK | Thou art no Atlas for so great a weight;<br>And, weakling, Warwick takes his gift again;<br>And Henry is my [[archetypes/king\|king]], Warwick his subject. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | But Warwick’s [[archetypes/king\|king]] is Edward’s prisoner,<br>And, gallant Warwick, do but answer this:<br>What is the [[concepts/body\|body]] when the head is off? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alas, that Warwick had no more forecast,<br>But whiles he thought to steal the single ten,<br>The [[archetypes/king\|king]] was slyly fingered from the deck. |
| *(didascalia)* | To Warwick You left poor Henry at the Bishop’s palace, |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And ten to one you’ll meet him in the Tower. |
| *(didascalia)* | ’Tis even so—to Warwick yet you are Warwick still. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Come, Warwick, take the [[motifs/time\|time]]—kneel down, kneel<br>down.<br>Nay, when? Strike now, or else the iron cools. |
| WARWICK | I had rather chop this hand off at a blow,<br>And with the other fling it at thy face,<br>Than bear so low a sail to strike to thee. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Sail how thou canst, have [[motifs/wind\|wind]] and tide thy friend,<br>This hand, fast wound about thy coal-black hair,<br>Shall, whiles thy head is warm and new cut off,<br>Write in the [[motifs/dust\|dust]] this sentence with thy [[motifs/blood\|blood]]:<br>‘[[motifs/wind\|Wind]]-changing Warwick now can change no more’. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], with a drummer and soldiers bearing colours |
| WARWICK | O cheerful colours! See where [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] comes. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], for Lancaster! |
| *(didascalia)* | [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] and his men pass over the stage and exeunt into the [[motifs/city\|city]] |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward)<br>The gates are open—let us enter too. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | So other foes may set upon our backs?<br>Stand we in good array, for they no [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]<br>Will issue out again and bid us battle.<br>If not, the [[motifs/city\|city]] being but of small defence,<br>We’ll quickly rouse the traitors in the same. |
| *(didascalia)* | WARWICK to [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], within |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | O, welcome, [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]—for we want thy help. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Marquis of Montague with a drummer and soldiers bearing colours |
| MONTAGUE | Montague, Montague, for Lancaster! |
| *(didascalia)* | Montague and his men pass over the stage and exeunt into the [[motifs/city\|city]] |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Thou and thy brother both shall bye this treason<br>Even with the dearest [[motifs/blood\|blood]] your bodies bear. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | The harder matched, the greater victory.<br>My mind presageth happy gain and conquest. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Somerset with a drummer and soldiers bearing colours |
| SOMERSET | Somerset, Somerset, for Lancaster! |
| *(didascalia)* | Somerset and his men pass over the stage and exeunt into the [[motifs/city\|city]] |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Two of thy name, both dukes of Somerset,<br>Have sold their lives unto the house of York—<br>And thou shalt be the third, an this sword hold. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] with a drummer and soldiers bearing colours |
| WARWICK | And lo, where George of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] sweeps along,<br>Of force enough to bid his brother battle;<br>With whom an upright zeal to right prevails<br>More than the [[concepts/nature\|nature]] of a brother’s [[motifs/love\|love]]. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], for Lancaster! |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Et tu, Brute—wilt thou stab Caesar too?<br>(To a trumpeter) A parley, sirra, to George of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound a parley. [[characters/richard\|Richard]] of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]] and George of |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] whisper together |
| WARWICK | Come, [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], come—thou wilt if Warwick call. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Father of Warwick, know you what this means? |
| *(didascalia)* | He takes his red [[motifs/rose\|rose]] out of his hat and throws it at Warwick |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Look—here I throw my infamy at thee!<br>I will not ruinate my father’s house,<br>Who gave his [[motifs/blood\|blood]] to lime the stones together,<br>And set up Lancaster. Why, trowest thou, Warwick,<br>That [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] is so harsh, so blunt, unnatural,<br>To bend the fatal instruments of war<br>Against his brother and his lawful [[archetypes/king\|king]]?<br>Perhaps thou wilt object my holy oath.<br>To keep that oath were more impiety<br>Than Jephthah, when he sacrificed his daughter.<br>I am so sorry for my trespass made<br>That, to deserve [[motifs/well\|well]] at my brothers’ hands,<br>I here proclaim myself thy mortal foe,<br>With resolution, wheresoe’er I meet thee—<br>As I will meet thee, if thou stir abroad—<br>To plague thee for thy foul misleading me.<br>And so, proud-hearted Warwick, I defy thee,<br>And to my brothers turn my blushing cheeks.<br>(To [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward)<br>Pardon me, Edward—I will make amends.<br>(To [[characters/richard\|Richard]])<br>And, [[characters/richard\|Richard]], do not frown upon my faults,<br>For I will henceforth be no more unconstant. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Now welcome more, and ten times more beloved,<br>Than if thou never hadst deserved our hate. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to George)<br>Welcome, good [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]]—this is brother-like. |
| WARWICK | (to George)<br>O, passing traitor—perjured and unjust! |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | What, Warwick, wilt thou leave the town and fight?<br>Or shall we beat the stones about thine ears? |
| *(didascalia)* | WARWICK aside |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Alas, I am not cooped here for defence.<br><br>(To [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward)<br>I will away towards Barnet presently,<br>And bid thee battle, Edward, if thou dar’st. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Yes, Warwick—Edward dares, and leads the way.<br>Lords, to the Held—Saint George and victory! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt below [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward and his company. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | March. The [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]] and his company<br>descend and follow |

### Atto 5, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Alarum and excursions. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward bringing forth the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]], wounded |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | So lie thou there. Die thou, and die our [[concepts/fear\|fear]]—<br>For Warwick was a bug that feared us all.<br>Now, Montague, sit fast—I seek for thee<br>That Warwick’s bones may keep thine company. Exit |
| WARWICK | Ah, who is nigh? Come to me, friend or foe,<br>And tell me who is victor, York or Warwick?<br>Why ask I that? My mangled [[concepts/body\|body]] shows,<br>My [[motifs/blood\|blood]], my want of strength, my sick heart shows,<br>That I must yield my [[concepts/body\|body]] to the earth<br>And by my fall the conquest to my foe.<br>Thus yields the cedar to the axe’s edge,<br>Whose arms gave shelter to the princely eagle,<br>Under whose shade the ramping [[motifs/lion\|lion]] slept,<br>Whose top-branch over-peered Jove’s spreading tree<br>And kept low shrubs from [[motifs/winter\|winter]]’s powerful [[motifs/wind\|wind]].<br>These eyes, that now are dimmed with [[motifs/death\|death]]’s black<br>veil,<br>Have been as piercing as the midday sun<br>To search the secret treasons of the world.<br>The wrinkles in my brows, now filled with [[motifs/blood\|blood]],<br>Were likened oft to kingly sepulchres—<br>For who lived [[archetypes/king\|king]], but I could dig his [[motifs/grave\|grave]]?<br>And who durst smile when Warwick bent his brow?<br>Lo now my glory smeared in [[motifs/dust\|dust]] and [[motifs/blood\|blood]].<br>My parks, my walks, my manors that I had,<br>Even now forsake me, and of all my lands<br>Is nothing left me but my [[concepts/body\|body]]’s length,<br>Why, what is pomp, rule, reign, but earth and [[motifs/dust\|dust]]?<br>And, live we how we can, yet die we must. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] and the Duke of Somerset |
| SOMERSET | Ah, Warwick, Warwick—wert thou as we are,<br>We might recover all our loss again.<br>The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] from France hath brought a puissant<br>power.<br>Even now we heard the news. Ah, couldst thou fly! |
| WARWICK | Why, then I would not fly. Ah, Montague,<br>If thou be there, sweet brother, take my hand,<br>And with thy lips keep in my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] a while.<br>Thou lov’st me not—for, brother, if thou didst,<br>Thy [[motifs/tears\|tears]] would wash this cold congealed [[motifs/blood\|blood]]<br>That glues my lips and will not let me speak.<br>Come quickly, Montague, or I am dead. |
| SOMERSET | Ah, Warwick—Montague hath breathed his last,<br>And to the latest gasp cried out for Warwick,<br>And said ‘Commend me to my valiant brother.’<br>And more he would have said, and more he spoke,<br>Which sounded like a canon in a vault,<br>That mote not be distinguished; but at last<br>I [[motifs/well\|well]] might hear, delivered with a groan,<br>‘O, farewell, Warwick.’ |
| WARWICK | Sweet rest his [[motifs/soul\|soul]]. Fly, lords, and save yourselves—<br>For Warwick bids you all farewell, to meet in heaven.<br>He dies |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | Away, away—to meet the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s great power!<br>Here they bear away Warwick’s [[concepts/body\|body]]. Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward in triumph, with [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], and soldiers |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Thus far our fortune keeps an upward course,<br>And we are graced with wreaths of victory.<br>But in the midst of this bright-shining day<br>I spy a black suspicious threatening cloud<br>That will encounter with our glorious sun<br>Ere he attain his easeful western bed.<br>I mean, my lords, those powers that the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]<br>Hath raised in Gallia have arrived our coast,<br>And, as we hear, march on to fight with us. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | A little gale will soon disperse that cloud,<br>And blow it to the source from whence it came.<br>Thy very beams will dry those vapours up,<br>For every cloud engenders not a [[motifs/storm\|storm]]. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] is valued thirty thousand strong,<br>And Somerset, with [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], fled to her.<br>If she have [[motifs/time\|time]] to breathe, be [[motifs/well\|well]] assured,<br>Her faction will be full as strong as ours. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | We are advertised by our loving friends<br>That they do hold their course toward Tewkesbury.<br>We, having now the best at Barnet field,<br>Will thither straight, for willingness rids way—<br>And, as we march, our strength will be augmented<br>In every county as we go along.<br>Strike up the drum, cry ‘[[concepts/courage\|Courage]]!’; and away. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. March Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. March. Enter [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]], Prince Edward, the Duke of Somerset, the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], and soldiers |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Great lords, wise men ne‘er sit and wail their loss,<br>But cheerly seek how to redress their harms.<br>What though the mast be now blown overboard,<br>The cable broke, the holding-anchor lost,<br>And half our sailors swallowed in the flood?<br>Yet lives our pilot still. Is’t meet that he<br>Should leave the helm and, like a fearful lad,<br>With tearful eyes add [[motifs/water\|water]] to the sea,<br>And give more strength to that which hath too much,<br>Whiles, in his moan, the [[motifs/ship\|ship]] splits on the rock<br>Which industry and [[concepts/courage\|courage]] might have saved?<br>Ah, what a shame; ah, what a fault were this.<br>Say Warwick was our anchor—what of that?<br>And Montague our top-mast—what of him?<br>Our slaughtered friends the tackles—what of these?<br>Why, is not [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] here another anchor?<br>And Somerset another goodly mast?<br>The friends of France our shrouds and tacklings?<br>And, though unskilful, why not Ned and I<br>For once allowed the skilful pilot’s charge?<br>We will not from the helm to sit and weep,<br>But keep our course, though the rough [[motifs/wind\|wind]] say no,<br>From shelves and rocks that threaten us with wreck.<br>As good to chide the waves as speak them fair.<br>And what is Edward but a ruthless sea?<br>What [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] but a quicksand of deceit?<br>And [[characters/richard\|Richard]] but a ragged fatal rock?<br>All these the enemies to our poor barque.<br>Say you can swim—alas, ’tis but a while;<br>Tread on the sand—why, there you quickly sink;<br>Bestride the rock—the tide will wash you off,<br>Or else you famish. That’s a threefold [[motifs/death\|death]].<br>This speak I, lords, to let you understand,<br>If case some one of you would fly from us,<br>That there’s no hoped-for [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] with the brothers York<br>More than with ruthless waves, with sands, and rocks.<br>Why, [[concepts/courage\|courage]] then—what cannot be avoided<br>’Twere childish weakness to lament or [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. |
| PRINCE EDWARD | Methinks a woman of this valiant spirit<br>Should, if a coward heard her speak these words,<br>Infuse his breast with magnanimity<br>And make him, naked, foil a man at arms.<br>I speak not this as doubting any here—<br>For did I but suspect a fearful man,<br>He should have leave to go away betimes,<br>Lest in our need he might infect another<br>And make him of like spirit to himself.<br>If any such be here—as God forbid—<br>Let him depart before we need his help. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | Women and children of so high a [[concepts/courage\|courage]],<br>And warriors faint—why, ’twere perpetual shame!<br>O brave young Prince, thy famous grandfather<br>Doth live again in thee! Long mayst thou live<br>To bear his image and renew his glories I |
| SOMERSET | And he that will not fight for such a [[concepts/hope\|hope]],<br>Go [[motifs/home\|home]] to bed, and like the owl by day,<br>If he arise, be mocked and wondered at. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Thanks, gentle Somerset; sweet [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], thanks. |
| PRINCE EDWARD | And take his thanks that yet hath nothing else. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| MESSENGER | Prepare you, lords, for Edward is at hand<br>Ready to fight—therefore be resolute. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | I thought no less. It is his policy<br>To haste thus fast to find us unprovided. |
| SOMERSET | But he’s deceived; we are in readiness. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | This cheers my heart, to see your forwardness. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | Here pitch our battle—hence we will not budge. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish and march. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward, [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], and George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], with soldiers |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (to his followers)<br>Brave followers, yonder stands the thorny wood<br>Which, by the heavens’ assistance and your strength,<br>Must by the roots be hewn up yet ere [[motifs/night\|night]].<br>I need not add more fuel to your [[motifs/fire\|fire]],<br>For [[motifs/well\|well]] I wot ye blaze to burn them out.<br>Give signal to the fight, and to it, lords. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | (to her followers)<br>Lords, knights, and gentlemen—what I should say<br>My [[motifs/tears\|tears]] gainsay; for every word I speak<br>Ye see I drink the [[motifs/water\|water]] of my eye.<br>Therefore, no more but this: Henry your sovereign<br>Is prisoner to the foe, his state usurped,<br>His realm a slaughter-house, his subjects slain,<br>His statutes cancelled, and his treasure spent—<br>And yonder is the wolf that makes this spoil.<br>You fight in justice; then in God’s name, lords,<br>Be valiant, and give signal to the fight. |
| *(didascalia)* | Alarum, retreat, excursions. Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward, [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], and George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] with [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]], the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], and the Duke of Somerset, guarded |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Now here a period of tumultuous broils.<br>Away with [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] to Hames [[settings/castle\|Castle]] straight;<br>For Somerset, off with his guilty head.<br>Go bear them hence—I will not hear them speak. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | For my part, I’ll not trouble thee with words. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit, guarded |
| SOMERSET | Nor I, but stoop with patience to my fortune. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit, guarded |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | So part we sadly in this troublous world<br>To meet with joy in sweet Jerusalem. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Is proclamation made that who finds Edward<br>Shall have a high reward and he his life? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | It is, and lo where youthful Edward comes. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince Edward, guarded |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Bring forth the gallant—let us hear him speak.<br>What, can so young a thorn begin to prick?<br>Edward, what satisfaction canst thou make<br>For bearing arms, for stirring up my subjects,<br>And all the trouble thou hast turned me to? |
| PRINCE EDWARD | Speak like a subject, proud ambitious York.<br>Suppose that I am now my father’s mouth—<br>Resign thy chair, and where I stand, kneel thou,<br>Whilst I propose the [[concepts/self\|self]]-same words to thee,<br>Which, traitor, thou wouldst have me answer to. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Ah, that thy father had been so resolved. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | That you might still have worn the petticoat<br>And ne’er have stolen the breech from Lancaster. |
| PRINCE EDWARD | Let Aesop [[forms/fable\|fable]] in a [[motifs/winter\|winter]]’s [[motifs/night\|night]]—<br>His currish riddles sorts not with this place. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | By heaven, brat, I’ll plague ye for that word. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Ay, thou wast born to be a plague to men. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | For God’s sake take away this captive scold. |
| PRINCE EDWARD | Nay, take away this scolding crookback rather. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Peace, wilful boy, or I will charm your tongue. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | (to Prince Edward)<br>Untutored lad, thou art too malapert. |
| PRINCE EDWARD | I know my duty—you are all undutiful.<br>Lascivious Edward, and thou, perjured George,<br>And thou, misshapen Dick—I tell ye all<br>I am your better, traitors as ye are,<br>And thou usurp’st my father’s right and mine. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Take that, the likeness of this railer here.<br>[[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward stabs Prince Edward |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Sprawl’st thou? Take that, to end thy agony.<br>[[characters/richard\|Richard]] stabs Prince Edward |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | And there’s for twitting me with perjury. |
| *(didascalia)* | George stabs Prince Edward, who dies |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | O, kill me too! |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Marry, and shall.<br>He offers to kill her |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Hold, [[characters/richard\|Richard]], hold—for we have done too much. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Why should she live to fill the world with words?<br>[[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]] faints |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | What—doth she swoon? Use means for her recovery. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (aside to George)<br>[[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], excuse me to the [[archetypes/king\|King]] my brother.<br>I’ll hence to [[settings/london\|London]] on a serious matter.<br>Ere ye come there, be sure to hear some news. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | (aside to [[characters/richard\|Richard]]) What? What? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (aside to George)<br>The Tower, the Tower. Exit |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | O Ned, sweet Ned—speak to thy mother, boy.<br>Canst thou not speak? O traitors, murderers!<br>They that stabbed Caesar shed no [[motifs/blood\|blood]] at all,<br>Did not offend, nor were not worthy blame,<br>If this foul deed were by to equal it.<br>He was a man—this, in respect, a [[archetypes/child\|child]];<br>And men ne’er spend their fury on a [[archetypes/child\|child]].<br>What’s worse than murderer that I may name it?<br>No, no, my heart will burst an if I speak;<br>And I will speak that so my heart may burst.<br>Butchers and villains! Bloody cannibals!<br>How sweet a plant have you untimely cropped!<br>You have no children, butchers; if you had,<br>The thought of them would have stirred up remorse.<br>But if you ever chance to have a [[archetypes/child\|child]],<br>Look in his youth to have him so cut off<br>As, deathsmen, you have rid this sweet young Prince! |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Away with her—go, bear her hence perforce. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Nay, never bear me hence—dispatch me here.<br>Here sheathe thy sword—I’ll pardon thee my [[motifs/death\|death]].<br>What? Wilt thou not? Then, [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], do it thou. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | By heaven, I will not do thee so much ease. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Good [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], do; sweet [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], do thou do it. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Didst thou not hear me swear I would not do it? |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Ay, but thou usest to forswear thyself.<br>‘Twas sin before, but now ’tis [[concepts/charity\|charity]].<br>What, wilt thou not? Where is that devil’s butcher,<br>Hard-favoured [[characters/richard\|Richard]]? [[characters/richard\|Richard]], where art thou?<br>Thou art not here. Murder is thy atms-deed—<br>Petitioners for [[motifs/blood\|blood]] thou ne‘er putt’st back. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Away, I say—t charge ye, bear her hence. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | So come to you and yours as to this Prince! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit, guarded |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Where’s [[characters/richard\|Richard]] gone? |
| *(didascalia)* | To [[settings/london\|London]] all in post—aside and as I guess, |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | To make a bloody supper in the Tower. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | He’s sudden if a thing comes in his head.<br>Now march we hence. Discharge the common sort<br>With pay and thanks, and let’s away to [[settings/london\|London]],<br>And see our gentle [[characters/queen\|Queen]] how [[motifs/well\|well]] she fares.<br>By this I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] she hath a son for me. Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 6

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter on the walls [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry the Sixth, reading a book, [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], and the Lieutenant of the Tower |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Good day, my lord. What, at your book so hard? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Ay, my good ford—‘my lord’, I should say, rather.<br>‘Tis sin to flatter; ‘good’ was little better.<br>‘Good [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]]’ and ‘good devil’ were alike,<br>And both preposterous—therefore not ‘good lord’. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to the Lieutenant)<br>Sirrah, leave us to ourselves. We must confer. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Lieutenant |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | So flies the reckless shepherd from the wolf;<br>So first the harmless sheep doth yield his fleece,<br>And next his throat unto the butcher’s knife.<br>What scene of [[motifs/death\|death]] hath Roscius now to act? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Suspicion always haunts the guilty mind;<br>The thief doth [[concepts/fear\|fear]] each bush an officer. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | The bird that hath been limèd in a bush<br>With trembling wings misdoubteth every bush.<br>And I, the hapless male to one sweet bird,<br>Have now the fatal object in my eye<br>Where my poor young was limed, was caught and<br>killed. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Why, what a peevish [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] was that of Crete,<br>That taught his son the office of a fowl!<br>And yet, for all his wings, the [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] was drowned. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | I, Daedalus; my poor boy, Icarus;<br>Thy father, Minos, that denied our course;<br>The sun that seared the wings of my sweet boy,<br>Thy brother Edward; and thyself, the sea,<br>Whose envious gulf did [[motifs/swallow\|swallow]] up his life.<br>Ah, kill me with thy weapon, not with words!<br>My breast can better brook thy dagger’s point<br>Than can my ears that tragic [[concepts/history\|history]].<br>But wherefore dost thou come? Is’t for my life? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Think’st thou I am an executioner? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | A persecutor I am sure thou art;<br>If murdering innocents be executing,<br>Why, then thou art an executioner. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Thy son I killed for his presumption. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Hadst thou been killed when first thou didst presume,<br>Thou hadst not lived to kill a son of mine.<br>And thus I prophesy: that many a thousand<br>Which now mistrust no parcel of my [[concepts/fear\|fear]],<br>And many an old man’s sigh, and many a widow‘s,<br>And many an [[archetypes/orphan\|orphan]]’s [[motifs/water\|water]]-standing eye—<br>Men for their sons’, wives for their husbands‘,<br>Orphans for their parents’ timeless [[motifs/death\|death]]—<br>Shall rue the hour that ever thou wast born.<br>The owl shrieked at thy birth—an evil sign;<br>The [[motifs/night\|night]]-crow cried, aboding luckless [[motifs/time\|time]];<br>Dogs howled, and hideous tempests shook down trees;<br>The raven rooked her on the chimney’s top;<br>And chatt’ring pies in dismal discords sung.<br>Thy mother felt more than a mother’s [[motifs/pain\|pain]],<br>And yet brought forth less than a mother’s [[concepts/hope\|hope]]—<br>To wit, an indigested and deformed lump,<br>Not like the fruit of such a goodly tree.<br>Teeth hadst thou in thy head when thou wast born,<br>To signify thou cam‘st to bite the world;<br>And if the rest be true which I have heard<br>Thou cam’st— |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] | I’ll hear no more. Die, [[archetypes/prophet\|prophet]], in thy speech,<br>He stabs him<br>For this, amongst the rest, was I ordained. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Ay, and for much more slaughter after this.<br>O, God forgive my sins, and pardon thee. He dies |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What—will the aspiring [[motifs/blood\|blood]] of Lancaster<br>Sink in the ground? I thought it would have mounted.<br>See how my sword weeps for the poor [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s [[motifs/death\|death]].<br>O, may such purple [[motifs/tears\|tears]] be alway shed<br>From those that wish the downfall of our house!<br>If any spark of life be yet remaining,<br>Down, down to hell, and say I sent thee thither—<br>He stabs him again<br>I that have neither pity, [[motifs/love\|love]], nor [[concepts/fear\|fear]].<br>Indeed, ‘tis true that Henry told me of,<br>For I have often heard my mother say<br>I came into the world with my legs forward.<br>Had I not reason, think ye, to make haste,<br>And seek their ruin that usurped our right?<br>The midwife wondered and the women cried<br>‘O, Jesus bless us, he is born with teeth !—<br>And so I was, which plainly signified<br>That I should snarl and bite and play the dog.<br>Then, since the heavens have shaped my [[concepts/body\|body]] so,<br>Let hell make crooked my mind to answer it.<br>I had no father, I am like no father;<br>I have no brother, I am like no brother;<br>And this word, ‘[[motifs/love\|love]]’, which greybeards call divine,<br>Be resident in men like one another<br>And not in me—I am myself alone.<br>[[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], beware; thou kept’st me from the light—<br>But I will sort a pitchy day for thee.<br>For I will buzz abroad such prophecies<br>That Edward shall be fearful of his life,<br>And then, to purge his [[concepts/fear\|fear]], I’ll be thy [[motifs/death\|death]].<br>Henry and his son are gone; thou, [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], art next;<br>And by one and one I will dispatch the rest,<br>Counting myself but bad till I be best.<br>I’ll throw thy [[concepts/body\|body]] in another room<br>And triumph, Henry, in thy day of doom. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit with the [[concepts/body\|body]] |

Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward, Lady Gray his [[characters/queen\|Queen]], George Duke off [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], the Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], a [[characters/nurse\|nurse]] carrying the infant Prince Edward, and attendants |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Once more we sit in England’s royal throne,<br>Repurchased with the [[motifs/blood\|blood]] of enemies.<br>What valiant foemen, like to [[motifs/autumn\|autumn]]’s corn,<br>Have we mowed down in tops of all their [[concepts/pride\|pride]]!<br>Three dukes of Somerset, threefold renowned<br>For hardy and undoubted champions;<br>Two Cliffords, as the father and the son;<br>And two Northumbertands—two braver men<br>Ne’er spurred their coursers at the trumpet’s sound.<br>With them, the two brave bears, Warwick and<br>Montague,<br>That in their [[motifs/chains\|chains]] fettered the kingly [[motifs/lion\|lion]]<br>And made the forest tremble when they roared.<br>Thus have we swept suspicion from our seat<br>And made our footstool of security.<br>(To Lady Gray)<br>Come hither, Bess, and let me kiss my boy.<br>The [[characters/nurse\|nurse]] brings forth the infant prince. [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward kisses him<br>Young Ned, for thee, thine uncles and myself<br>Have in our armours watched the [[motifs/winter\|winter]]’s [[motifs/night\|night]],<br>Went all afoot in [[motifs/summer\|summer]]’s scalding heat,<br>That thou mightst repossess the [[motifs/crown\|crown]] in peace;<br>And of our labours thou shalt reap the gain. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (aside)<br>I’ll blast his harvest, an your head were laid;<br>For yet I am not looked on in the world.<br>This shoulder was ordained so thick to heave;<br>And heave it shall some weight or break my back.<br>Work thou the way, and thou shalt execute. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] and [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]], [[motifs/love\|love]] my lovely [[characters/queen\|queen]];<br>And kiss your princely nephew, brothers, both. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | The duty that I owe unto your majesty<br>I seal upon the lips of this sweet babe.<br>He kisses the infant prince |
| LADY GRAY | Thanks, noble [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]]—worthy brother, thanks. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And that I [[motifs/love\|love]] the tree from whence thou sprang’st,<br>Witness the loving kiss I give the fruit.<br>He kisses the infant prince<br>(Aside) To say the [[concepts/truth\|truth]], so Judas kissed his master,<br>And cried ‘All hail!’ whenas he meant all harm. |

| *(didascalia)* | Sound drums and trumpets—fareweti, sour annoy! |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | For here, I [[concepts/hope\|hope]], begins our lasting joy. |

For York!’ Then [[characters/queen-margaret\|Queen Margaret]], Prince Edward, [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] and Somerset are all taken prisoner. Flourish, and enter all again |

| BY WILLIAM SHAKESPEARE AND | OTHERS |
| THE | play printed here first appeared in the 1623 Folio, as The First Part of [[characters/henry-vi\|Henry VI]]; it tells the beginning of the story that is continued in The First Part of the Contention and in [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]]. Although in narrative sequence it belongs before those plays, there is good reason to believe that it was written after them. It is probably the ‘new’ play referred to as ‘harey the vj’ in the record of its performance on 3 March 1592 by Lord Strange’s Men. The box-office takings of £3 16s. 8d. were a record for the season, and the play was acted another fifteen times during the following ten months. Its success is mentioned in Thomas Nashe’s satirical pamphlet Piers Penniless, published later in 1592. Defending the drama against moralistic attacks, Nashe claims that plays based on ‘our English chronicles’ celebrate ‘our forefathers’ valiant acts’ and set them up as a ‘reproof to these degenerate effeminate days of ours’. By way of illustration he alludes specifically to the exploits of [[characters/lord-talbot\|Lord Talbot]], the principal English warrior in [[characters/henry-vi\|Henry VI]] Part One: ‘How would it have joyed brave Talbot, the terror of the French, to think that after he had lain two hundred years in his tomb he should triumph again on the stage, and have his bones new-embalmed with the [[motifs/tears\|tears]] of ten thousand spectators at least, at several times, who in the tragedian that represents his person imagine they behold him fresh bleeding!’ Nashe may have had personal reasons to puff this play: a variety of evidence suggests that Shakespeare wrote it in collaboration with at least two other authors; Nashe himself was probably responsible for Act 1. The passages most confidently attributed to Shakespeare are Act 2, Scene 4 and Act 4, Scene 2 to the [[motifs/death\|death]] of Talbot at 4.7.32.<br>A mass of material, some derived from ‘English chronicles’, some invented, is packed into this play. It opens impressively with the funeral of [[characters/henry-v\|Henry V]], celebrated for unifying England and subjugating France; but his nobles are at loggerheads even over his coffin, and news rapidly arrives of serious losses in France. The rivalry displayed here between Humphrey, [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]]—Protector of the infant [[characters/henry-vi\|Henry VI]]—and Henry Beaufort, Bishop of Winchester, plays an important part in both this play and The Contention, as does the conflict between [[characters/richard\|Richard]], [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]], and the houses of Somerset and Suffolk; in the Temple [[motifs/garden\|Garden]] scene (2.4), invented by Shakespeare, York’s and Somerset’s supporters symbolize their respective loyalties by plucking [[motifs/white-and-red\|white and red]] roses. Their dissension weakens England’s military strength, but she has a great [[archetypes/hero\|hero]] in [[characters/lord-talbot\|Lord Talbot]], whose nobility as a warrior is pitted against the treachery of the French, led by [[archetypes/king\|King]] Charles and [[characters/joan-la-pucelle\|Joan la Pucelle]] (Joan of Arc), here—following the chronicles—portrayed as a [[characters/witch\|witch]] and a whore. Historical facts are freely manipulated: Joan was burnt in 1431, though the play’s authors have her take part in a battle of 1451 in which Talbot’s [[motifs/death\|death]] is brought forward by two years. The play ends with an uneasy peace between England and France. |

<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

[[characters/richard|RICHARD]] [[characters/duke-of-york|DUKE OF YORK]]
(3 [[characters/henry-vi|HENRY VI]])
THE play printed in the 1623 Folio as The Third Part of Henry the Sixth, with the [[motifs/death|Death]] of the [[characters/duke-of-york|Duke of York]] was described on the title-page of its first, unauthoritative publication in 1595 as The True [[forms/tragedy|Tragedy]] of [[characters/richard|Richard]], [[characters/duke-of-york|Duke of York]], and the [[motifs/death|Death]] of Good [[archetypes/king|King]] Henry the Sixth, with the whole Contention between the two houses Lancaster and York. It is clearly a continuation of The First Part of the Contention, taking up the story where that play had ended, with the aspirations of [[characters/richard|Richard]], [[characters/duke-of-york|Duke of York]] to the English throne, and was probably composed immediately afterwards.
The final scenes of The First Part of the Contention briefly introduce two of York’s sons, Edward (the eldest) and [[characters/richard|Richard]] (already described as a ‘foul, indigested lump, | As crooked in . . . manners as [in] shape’). They, along with their brothers [[characters/edmund|Edmund]], Earl of Rutland, and George (later Duke of [[characters/clarence|Clarence]]), figure more prominently in [[characters/richard|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-york|Duke of York]]. The first scenes show York apparently fulfilling his [[concepts/ambition|ambition]], as [[characters/henry-vi|Henry VI]] weakly cedes his rights to the throne after his [[motifs/death|death]]; but [[characters/queen-margaret|Queen Margaret]] leads an army against York, and, when he is captured, personally taunts him with news of the murder of his youngest son, stabs York to [[motifs/death|death]], and commands that his head be ‘set on York gates’. (This powerful scene includes the line ‘O tiger’s heart wrapped in a woman’s hide’, paraphrased by Robert Greene before September 1592, which establishes the upward limit of the play’s date.)
Though [[characters/richard|Richard]] of York dies early in the action, the remainder of the play centres on his sons’ efforts (aided by Warwick’s politic schemings) to avenge his [[motifs/death|death]] and to establish the dominance of Yorkists over Lancastrians. The balance of power shifts frequently, and the brothers’ alliance crumbles, but finally [[characters/queen-margaret|Queen Margaret]], with her French allies, is defeated and captured, and [[characters/richard|Richard]] of York’s surviving sons avenge their father’s [[motifs/death|death]] by killing her son, Edward, before her eyes. [[characters/richard|Richard]] of [[characters/gloucester|Gloucester]] starts to clear his way to the throne by murdering ‘Good [[archetypes/king|King]] Henry’ in the Tower, and the play ends with the new [[archetypes/king|King]] [[characters/edward-iv|Edward IV]] exulting in his ‘country’s peace and brothers’ loves’ while [[characters/richard|Richard]] makes clear to the audience that Edward’s [[concepts/self|self]]-confidence is ill-founded.
Though the play is loud and strife-ridden with war, power politics, and personal [[concepts/ambition|ambition]], a concern with humane values emerges in the subtle and touching continuing portrayal of the quietist [[characters/henry-vi|Henry VI]], a saintly [[archetypes/fool|fool]] who meditates on the superiority of humble contentment to regal misery in an emblematic scene (2.5) that epitomizes the [[forms/tragedy|tragedy]] of civil strife.
[[characters/richard|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-york|Duke of York]], like The First Part of the Contention, draws extensively on English chronicle [[concepts/history|history]]. Historically, the period of the action covers about sixteen years (1455 to 1471), but events are telescoped and rearranged; for instance, the opening scenes move rapidly from the Battle of St Albans (1445) to York’s [[motifs/death|death]] (1460); the future [[characters/richard-iii|Richard III]] was only three years old, and living abroad, at the [[motifs/time|time]] of this opening battle in which he takes an active part; and [[characters/richard|Richard]]’s murder of Henry owes more to legend than to fact.

The True [[forms/tragedy|Tragedy]] of [[characters/richard|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-york|Duke of York]] and the Good [[archetypes/king|King]] Henry the Sixth

### Atto 1, Scena 1

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Un trono. Squillo d’allarme. Entrano [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] Plantageneto, [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]], i suoi due figli Edoardo, conte di March, e [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] il Gobbo, il duca di Norfolk, il marchese di Montague, e il [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|conte di Warwick]], con tamburini e soldati. Tutti portano rose bianche sui cappelli |
| WARWICK | Mi [[concepts/wonder\|domando]] come il [[archetypes/king\|re]] sia scampato alle nostre mani? |
| YORK | Mentre inseguivamo i cavalieri del nord,<br>egli furtivo si dileguò lasciando i suoi uomini;<br>allora il gran signore di Northumberland,<br>il cui orecchio guerriero mai tollerò ritirata,<br>rianimò l’esercito che cedeva; ed egli stesso,<br>lord [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]] e lord Stafford, tutti affiancati,<br>caricarono il fronte della nostra schiera, e, sfondando,<br>furono trucidati dalle spade di soldati comuni. |
| EDOARDO | Il padre di lord Stafford, [[characters/duke-of-buckingham\|duca di Buckingham]],<br>o è ucciso o è ferito a morte.<br>Gli spaccai la visiera con un colpo diritto.<br>Che questo sia vero, padre, guardate il suo [[motifs/blood\|sangue]].<br>Mostra una spada sanguinante |
| *(didascalia)* | MONTAGUE a York |
| EDOARDO | E, fratello, ecco il [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] del conte di Wiltshire,<br>Mostra una spada sanguinante<br>che affrontai quando le schiere si scontrarono. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | (alla testa di Somerset, che mostra) Parla tu per me, e di’ loro ciò che ho fatto. |
| YORK | [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] ha meritato più di tutti i miei figli.<br>(Alla testa) Ma è morta vostra grazia, mio signore di<br>Somerset? |
| NORFOLK | Tale sorte abbia tutta la stirpe di [[characters/john-of-gaunt\|Giovanni di Gaunt]]. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | Così [[concepts/hope\|spero]] di scuotere la testa di [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico. |
| *(didascalia)* | Solleva in alto la testa, poi la getta a terra |
| WARWICK | E così anch’io, vittorioso principe di York.<br>Prima di vederti insediato su quel trono<br>che ora la casa di Lancaster usurpa,<br>giuro per il cielo che questi occhi non si chiuderanno.<br>Questo è il palazzo del [[archetypes/king\|re]] pauroso,<br>e questo (indicando il trono), il regale<br>seggio—prendilo, York,<br>perché è tuo, e non degli eredi di [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico. |
| YORK | Assistimi allora, dolce Warwick, e lo farò,<br>ché qui siamo entrati a viva forza. |
| NORFOLK | Tutti ti assisteremo—chi fugge morirà. |
| YORK | Grazie, gentile Norfolk. Restate presso di me, miei signori<br>e soldati—restate, e alloggiate presso di me questa [[motifs/night\|notte]].<br>Salgono sul trono |
| WARWICK | E quando viene il [[archetypes/king\|re]], non usargli violenza<br>a meno che non cerchi di cacciarti fuori a forza. |
| *(didascalia)* | I soldati si ritirano |
| YORK | La [[characters/queen\|regina]] tiene oggi qui il suo Parlamento,<br>ma poco pensa che saremo del suo consiglio;<br>con parole o con colpi conquistiamo qui il nostro diritto. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | Armati come siamo, restiamo dentro questa casa. |
| WARWICK | ‘Il Parlamento Sanguinario’ sarà chiamato questo,<br>se Plantageneto, [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]], non sarà [[archetypes/king\|re]],<br>e il timido Enrico non sarà deposto, la cui codardia<br>ci ha resi lo scherno dei nostri nemici. |
| YORK | Allora non lasciatemi, miei signori. Siate risoluti—<br>intendo prendere possesso del mio diritto. |
| WARWICK | Né il [[archetypes/king\|re]] né chi più l’ama—<br>il più superbo fra quanti sostengono Lancaster—<br>osa muovere un’ala se Warwick scuote i suoi [[motifs/bells\|campanelli]].<br>Io pianterò Plantageneto: lo sradichi chi osa.<br>Decidi, [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]]—reclama la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] d’Inghilterra. |
| *(didascalia)* | York siede sul trono. |
| *(didascalia)* | Squilli. Entrano [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico, lord [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]], i conti |
| WARWICK | di Northumberland e Westmorland, il duca di |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeter, e gli altri. Tutti portano rose rosse nei |
| *(didascalia)* | loro cappelli |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Miei signori, guardate dove siede il tenace [[archetypes/rebel\|ribelle]]—<br>proprio sul trono! Certo egli intende,<br>sostenuto dal potere di Warwick, quel falso pari,<br>aspirare alla [[motifs/crown\|corona]] e regnare da [[archetypes/king\|re]].<br>[[characters/earl-of-northumberland\|Conte di Northumberland]], egli uccise tuo padre—<br>e il tuo, lord [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]]—e voi entrambi avete giurato<br>[[concepts/revenge\|vendetta]]<br>contro lui, i suoi figli, i suoi favoriti e i suoi amici. |
| NORTHUMBERLAND | Se non lo faccio, si vendichi il cielo su di me. |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | La [[concepts/hope\|speranza]] di ciò fa che [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]] porti lutto nell’acciaio. |
| WESTMORLAND | Che? Dobbiamo sopportarlo? Strappiamolo giù.<br>Il cuore mi brucia d’ira—non lo tollero. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Sii paziente, gentile conte di Westmorland. |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | La pazienza è per i codardi, come lui (indicando York).<br>Non oserebbe sedere là se vostro padre vivesse.<br>Mio grazioso signore, qui nel Parlamento<br>assaliamo la famiglia di York. |
| NORTHUMBERLAND | [[motifs/well\|Bene]] hai parlato, cugino, sia così. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Ah, non sapete che la [[motifs/city\|città]] li favorisce,<br>e che hanno schiere di soldati a un loro cenno? |
| EXETER | Ma ucciso il duca, fuggiranno in fretta. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Lungi dal cuore di Enrico questo pensiero,<br>di fare un macello della Casa del Parlamento.<br>Cugino di Exeter, cipigli, parole e minacce<br>saranno la guerra che Enrico intende usare.<br>(A York) Tu, fazioso [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]], scendi dal mio<br>trono<br>e inginocchiati per grazia e [[concepts/mercy\|pietà]] ai miei piedi.<br>Io sono il tuo sovrano. |
| YORK | Sono io il tuo. |
| EXETER | Vergogna, scendi—egli ti fece [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]]. |
| YORK | Era mia eredità, come lo era la contea. |
| EXETER | Tuo padre fu un traditore della [[motifs/crown\|corona]]. |
| WARWICK | Exeter, tu sei un traditore della [[motifs/crown\|corona]]<br>seguendo questo Enrico usurpatore. |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | Chi dovrebbe seguire se non il suo naturale [[archetypes/king\|re]]? |
| WARWICK | Vero, [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]], e quello è [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | (a York)<br>E io starò in piedi e tu sederai sul mio trono? |
| YORK | Deve e sarà così—accontèntati. |
| WARWICK | (a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico)<br>Sii duca di Lancaster, lascia che egli sia [[archetypes/king\|re]]. |
| WESTMORLAND | Egli è [[archetypes/king\|re]] e duca di Lancaster insieme—<br>e questo il signore di Westmorland sosterrà. |
| WARWICK | E Warwick lo confuterà. Dimenticate<br>che noi siamo quelli che vi cacciarono dal campo,<br>e uccisero i vostri padri, e, con le insegne al vento,<br>marciarono per la [[motifs/city\|città]] fino alle porte del palazzo. |
| NORTHUMBERLAND | Sì, Warwick, lo ricordo a mio dolore,<br>e, per la sua [[motifs/soul\|anima]], tu e la tua casa ne piangerete. |
| WESTMORLAND | (a York)<br>Plantageneto, di te, e di questi tuoi figli,<br>dei tuoi parenti e dei tuoi amici, avrò più vite<br>di quante gocce di [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] furono nelle vene di mio padre. |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | (a Warwick)<br>Non insistere più, ché invece di parole<br>io ti manderò, Warwick, un tale messaggero<br>che [[concepts/revenge\|vendicherà]] la sua [[motifs/death\|morte]] prima ch’io mi muova. |
| *(didascalia)* | WARWICK a York |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | Povero [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]], come disprezzo le sue vane minacce. |
| *(didascalia)* | YORK a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | Volete che mostriamo il nostro titolo alla [[motifs/crown\|corona]]?<br>Se no, le nostre spade lo perorino sul campo. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Che titolo hai tu, traditore, alla [[motifs/crown\|corona]]?<br>Tuo padre fu, come tu sei, [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]];<br>tuo nonno, Roger Mortimer, conte di March.<br>Io sono il figlio di Enrico Quinto,<br>che piegò il [[characters/dauphin\|Delfino]] e i Francesi<br>e s’impadronì delle loro città e province. |
| WARWICK | Non parlare di Francia, poiché l’hai perduta tutta. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | La perse il lord Protettore, non io.<br>Quando fui incoronato, avevo appena nove mesi. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | Ora sei grande abbastanza, e tuttavia, mi pare, perdi.<br>(A York) Padre, strappa la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] dalla testa<br>dell’[[archetypes/usurper\|usurpatore]]. |
| EDOARDO | (a York)<br>Dolce padre, fatelo—ponetela sul vostro capo. |
| MONTAGUE | (a York)<br>Buon fratello, come ami e [[concepts/honour\|onori]] le armi,<br>battiamoci e non stiamo a cavillare così. |
| *(didascalia)* | Suonate tamburi e trombe, e il [[archetypes/king\|re]] fuggirà. |
| YORK | Figli, pace! |
| *(didascalia)* | NORTHUMBERLAND |
| YORK | Pace, tu—e lascia parlare [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Ah, York, perché cerchi di depormi?<br>Non siamo entrambi Plantageneti per nascita,<br>e da due fratelli discesi in linea diretta?<br>Supponi che per diritto ed equità tu sia [[archetypes/king\|re]]—<br>credi che io lascerò il mio trono regale,<br>dove sedettero il mio avo e mio padre?<br>No—prima la guerra spopolerà questo mio regno;<br>sì, e le loro insegne, spesso portate in Francia,<br>e ora in Inghilterra a gran dolore del nostro cuore,<br>saranno il mio sudario. Perché vi sgomentate, signori?<br>Il mio titolo è buono, e ben migliore del suo. |
| WARWICK | Provalo, Enrico, e sarai [[archetypes/king\|re]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Enrico Quarto ottenne la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] per conquista. |
| YORK | Fu per ribellione contro il suo [[archetypes/king\|re]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO a parte |
| YORK | Non so che dire—il mio titolo è debole.<br>(A York) Dimmi, non può un [[archetypes/king\|re]] adottare un erede? |
| YORK | E allora? |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | E se può, allora io sono legittimo [[archetypes/king\|re]]—<br>ché [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]], al cospetto di molti signori,<br>rassegnò la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] a Enrico Quarto,<br>del quale mio padre fu erede, e io sono il suo. |
| YORK | Si [[motifs/rose\|levò]] contro di lui, che era il suo sovrano,<br>e lo costrinse a rassegnare la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] per forza. |
| WARWICK | Supponete, signori, che l’abbia fatto non costretto—<br>credete che sarebbe pregiudizievole alla sua [[motifs/crown\|corona]]? |
| EXETER | No, perché non poteva così rassegnare la [[motifs/crown\|corona]]<br>senza che l’erede più prossimo succedesse e regnasse. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Sei tu contro di noi, [[characters/duke-of-exeter\|duca di Exeter]]? |
| EXETER | Suo è il diritto, e perciò perdonatemi. |
| YORK | Perché sussurrate, signori, e non rispondete? |
| *(didascalia)* | EXETER a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico |
| YORK | La mia [[concepts/conscience\|coscienza]] mi dice che egli è legittimo [[archetypes/king\|re]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO a parte |
| YORK | Tutti si ribelleranno a me e si volgeranno a lui. |
| NORTHUMBERLAND | (a York)<br>Plantageneto, per ogni pretesa che tu avanzi,<br>non pensare che Enrico sarà così deposto. |
| WARWICK | Deposto sarà, a dispetto di tutti. |
| NORTHUMBERLAND | T’inganni—non è la tua forza del sud,<br>d’Essex, Norfolk, Suffolk, né di [[characters/kent\|Kent]],<br>che ti rende così presuntuoso e superbo,<br>che possa innalzare il duca a mio dispetto. |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | [[archetypes/king\|Re]] Enrico, sia il tuo titolo giusto o torto,<br>lord [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]] giura di combattere in tua difesa.<br>Si spalanchi quel suolo e mi [[motifs/swallow\|inghiotta]] vivo<br>là dove m’inginocchiassi a chi uccise mio padre. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | O [[characters/clifford\|Clifford]], come le tue parole rianimano il mio cuore! |
| YORK | Enrico di Lancaster, rassegna la tua [[motifs/crown\|corona]].<br>Che mormorate, o che cospirate, signori? |
| WARWICK | Fate giustizia a questo principesco [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]],<br>o riempirò la casa di uomini armati<br>e sopra il trono, dove ora egli siede,<br>scriverò il suo titolo con [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] usurpatore.<br>Batte il piede e i soldati si<br><br>mostrano |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Mio signore di Warwick, ascoltatemi una sola parola—<br>lasciate ch’io regni da [[archetypes/king\|re]] per questa mia vita. |
| YORK | Conferma la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] a me e ai miei eredi,<br>e regnerai in pace mentre vivi. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Sono contento. [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] Plantageneto,<br>godi il regno dopo il mio trapasso. |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | Che torto è questo al principe vostro figlio? |
| WARWICK | Che bene è questo per l’Inghilterra e per lui stesso? |
| WESTMORLAND | Vile, pauroso e disperato Enrico. |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | Come hai offeso te stesso e noi insieme? |
| WESTMORLAND | Non posso restare a udire questi articoli. |
| NORTHUMBERLAND | Né io. |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | Venite, cugino, andiamo a dire alla [[characters/queen\|regina]] queste nuove. |
| WESTMORLAND | (a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico)<br>Addio, [[archetypes/king\|re]] pusillanime e degenere,<br>nel cui freddo [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] non dimora scintilla d’[[concepts/honour\|onore]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce con i suoi soldati |
| NORTHUMBERLAND | (a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico)<br>Sii preda della casa di York,<br>e muori in catene per questo atto non virile. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce con i suoi soldati |
| [[characters/clifford\|CLIFFORD]] | (a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico)<br>In guerra terribile possa tu essere vinto,<br>o vivere in pace, abbandonato e disprezzato. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce con i suoi soldati |
| WARWICK | (a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico)<br>Volgiti da questa parte, Enrico, e non badare a loro. |
| EXETER | (a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico)<br>Cercano [[concepts/revenge\|vendetta]] e perciò non cederanno. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Ah, Exeter. |
| WARWICK | Perché sospirate, mio signore? |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Non per me, lord Warwick, ma per mio figlio,<br>che snaturatamente diserederò.<br>Ma sia come sia. (A York) Io qui vincolo<br>la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] a te e ai tuoi eredi per sempre,<br>a condizione che qui tu presti giuramento<br>di cessare questa guerra civile, e mentre io vivo<br>di [[concepts/honour\|onorarmi]] come tuo [[archetypes/king\|re]] e sovrano,<br>e né per tradimento né per ostilità<br>di cercare d’abbattermi e di regnare tu stesso. |
| YORK | Questo giuramento presto volentieri e lo compirò. |
| WARWICK | Viva [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico. (A York) Plantageneto, abbraccialo. |
| *(didascalia)* | York scende. Enrico e York si abbracciano |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | (a York)<br>E viva tu, e questi tuoi arditi figli. |
| YORK | Ora York e Lancaster sono riconciliati. |
| EXETER | Maledetto colui che cerca di farne nemici. Sennet. Qui il séguito di York scende dal trono |
| YORK | (a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico) |
| *(didascalia)* | Addio, mio grazioso signore, andrò al mio [[settings/castle\|castello]]. Escono York, Edoardo e [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]], con soldati |
| WARWICK | E io terrò [[settings/london\|Londra]] coi miei soldati. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce Con i soldati |
| NORFOLK | E io a Norfolk coi miei seguaci. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce Con i soldati |
| MONTAGUE | E io al mare da cui venni. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce con i soldati |
| *(didascalia)* | E io con pena e dolore alla [[settings/court\|corte]]. [[archetypes/king\|Re]] Enrico ed Exeter si volgono per uscire. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[characters/queen-margaret\|la regina Margherita]] e il principe Edoardo |
| EXETER | Ecco viene la [[characters/queen\|regina]], il cui sguardo tradisce la sua ira. Me ne andrò di soppiatto. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Exeter, e anch’io. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|REGINA MARGHERITA]] | No, non allontanarti da me—io ti seguirò. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Sii paziente, gentile [[characters/queen\|regina]], e resterò. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|REGINA MARGHERITA]] | Chi può esser paziente in tali estremi?<br>Ah, uomo miserabile, fossi morta vergine<br>e non t’avessi mai visto, mai partorito un figlio,<br>vedendo che ti sei mostrato padre così snaturato.<br>Ha egli meritato di perdere così il suo diritto di nascita?<br>Se solo l’avessi amato la metà di quanto [[motifs/well\|bene]] l’amai io,<br>o sentito quel [[motifs/pain\|dolore]] che io sentii per lui un tempo,<br>o nutrito lui come io feci col mio [[motifs/blood\|sangue]],<br>avresti lasciato là il [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] più caro del tuo cuore<br>anziché fare di quel selvaggio duca il tuo erede<br>e diseredare il tuo unico figlio. |
| PRINCIPE EDOARDO | Padre, non potete diseredarmi.<br>Se voi siete [[archetypes/king\|re]], perché non dovrei succedervi? |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Perdonami, Margherita; perdonami, dolce figlio—<br>il [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|conte di Warwick]] e il duca mi costrinsero. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|REGINA MARGHERITA]] | Costretto? Sei tu [[archetypes/king\|re]], e vuoi esser costretto?<br>Mi vergogno d’udirti parlare! Ah, timoroso miserabile,<br>hai rovinato te stesso, tuo figlio e me,<br>e dato alla casa di York tale libertà<br>che regnerai solo per loro tolleranza.<br>Vincolare lui e i suoi eredi alla [[motifs/crown\|corona]]—<br>che è, se non farti il sepolcro<br>e strisciarvi dentro molto prima del tuo [[motifs/time\|tempo]]?<br>Warwick è Cancelliere e signore di Calais;<br>il severo Falconbridge comanda gli stretti mari;<br>il duca è fatto Protettore del Regno;<br>e tu sarai al sicuro? Tale sicurezza trova<br>l’[[motifs/lamb\|agnello]] tremante circondato dai lupi.<br>Fossi stata là, io che sono una debole donna,<br>i soldati m’avrebbero gettata sulle loro picche<br>prima ch’io consentissi a quell’atto.<br>Ma tu preferisci la tua vita al tuo [[concepts/honour\|onore]].<br>E poiché fai così, io qui mi separo<br>sia dalla tua mensa, Enrico, sia dal tuo letto,<br>finché quell’atto del Parlamento non sia revocato<br>per cui mio figlio è diseredato.<br>I signori del nord che hanno abiurato le tue insegne<br>seguiranno le mie, se una volta le vedranno spiegate—<br>e spiegate saranno, a tua turpe vergogna<br>e a totale rovina della casa di York.<br>Così ti lascio. (Al principe Edoardo) Vieni, figlio,<br>andiamo.<br>Il nostro esercito è pronto—vieni, andremo dietro a loro. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Resta, gentile Margherita, e ascoltami parlare. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|REGINA MARGHERITA]] | Hai già parlato troppo. |
| *(didascalia)* | Al principe Edoardo Vattene. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Gentile figlio Edoardo, resterai con me? |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|REGINA MARGHERITA]] | Sì, per essere assassinato dai suoi nemici. |
| PRINCIPE EDOARDO | (a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico)<br>Quando tornerò vittorioso dal campo,<br>vedrò vostra grazia. Fino allora, la seguirò. |
| [[characters/queen-margaret\|REGINA MARGHERITA]] | Vieni, figlio, via—non possiamo indugiare così. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce col principe Edoardo |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Povera [[characters/queen\|regina]], come l’[[motifs/love\|amore]] per me e per suo figlio<br>l’ha fatta erompere in parole di rabbia.<br>Possa ella vendicarsi di quel duca odioso,<br>il cui spirito altero, alato di [[motifs/desire\|desiderio]],<br>insidierà la mia [[motifs/crown\|corona]], e, come aquila digiuna,<br>si pascerà della carne mia e di mio figlio.<br>La perdita di quei tre signori mi tormenta il cuore.<br>Scriverò loro e li pregherò cortesemente.<br>Venite, cugino, voi sarete il messaggero. |
| EXETER | E io, [[concepts/hope\|spero]], li riconcilierò tutti. |
| *(didascalia)* | Squilli. Escono |

### Atto 1, Scena 2

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]], Edoardo conte di March, e il marchese di Montague |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | Fratello, benché sia il più giovane, lasciate parlare me. |
| EDOARDO | No, io saprò meglio fare l’oratore. |
| MONTAGUE | Ma io ho ragioni forti e cogenti. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra il [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]] |
| YORK | Ebbene, che c’è, figli e fratello—in contesa?<br>Qual è la vostra lite? Come è cominciata? |
| EDOARDO | Nessuna lite, ma una piccola disputa. |
| YORK | Intorno a che? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | Intorno a ciò che riguarda vostra grazia e noi—<br>la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] d’Inghilterra, padre, che è vostra. |
| YORK | Mia, ragazzo? Non prima che [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico sia morto. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | Il vostro diritto non dipende dalla sua vita o [[motifs/death\|morte]]. |
| EDOARDO | Ora voi siete l’erede—perciò godetene ora.<br>Se lasciate alla casa di Lancaster il tempo di respirare,<br>alla fine essa vi sopravanzerà, padre. |
| YORK | Ho prestato giuramento che egli regnasse in pace. |
| EDOARDO | Ma per un regno ogni giuramento può esser rotto.<br>Io romperei mille giuramenti per regnare un anno. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | (a York)<br>No—Dio non voglia che vostra grazia sia spergiura. |
| YORK | Lo sarò se reclamerò con guerra aperta. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | Proverò il contrario, se volete udirmi parlare. |
| YORK | Non puoi, figlio—è impossibile. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | Un giuramento non ha valore se non è prestato<br>davanti a un magistrato vero e legittimo<br>che abbia autorità su colui che giura.<br>Enrico non ne aveva alcuna, ma usurpò il posto.<br>Dunque, poiché fu lui a farvi deporre,<br>il vostro giuramento, mio signore, è vano e frivolo.<br>Perciò alle armi—e, padre, pensate soltanto<br>quanto dolce cosa sia portare una [[motifs/crown\|corona]], |
| *(didascalia)* | dentro il cui cerchio è l’Eliso |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] | e tutto ciò che i poeti fingono di beatitudine e gioia.<br>Perché indugiamo così? Non posso riposare<br>finché la bianca [[motifs/rose\|rosa]] che porto non sia tinta<br>nel tepido [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] del cuore di Enrico. |
| YORK | [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]], basta!
