Tengo per codardia<br>restar diffidente là dove un nobile cuore<br>ha impegnato una mano aperta in segno d’[[motifs/love\|amore]],<br>altrimenti potrei pensare che [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], fratello d’Edoardo,<br>fosse solo un finto amico dei nostri disegni.<br>Ma vieni, dolce [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], mia figlia sarà tua.<br>E ora che resta se non che, sotto la copertura della [[motifs/night\|notte]],<br>essendo tuo fratello accampato senza cautela,<br>i suoi soldati sparsi nelle città d’intorno,<br>e assistito solo da una semplice guardia,<br>possiamo sorprenderlo e prenderlo a nostro piacere?<br>I nostri esploratori han trovato l’impresa molto facile;<br>sì che, come [[characters/ulysses\|Ulisse]] e il gagliardo Diomede<br>con astuzia e valore s’insinuarono nelle tende di Reso<br>e ne portarono via i fatali destrieri di Tracia,<br>così noi, [[motifs/well\|ben]] coperti dal nero mantello della [[motifs/night\|notte]],<br>possiamo all’improvviso abbattere la guardia d’Edoardo<br>e afferrare lui stesso—non dico ‘trucidarlo’,<br>ché intendo soltanto sorprenderlo.<br>Voi che volete seguirmi in questo tentativo,<br>acclamate col vostro capo il nome di Enrico.<br>Tutti gridano ‘Enrico’<br>Ebbene, allora avviamoci in silenzio,<br>per Warwick e i suoi amici, Dio e san Giorgio! |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_3" data-title="Richard Duke of York (3 Henry VI) — Act IV, Scene 3" data-chapter="Scene 3" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,motif/night,motif/sleep,concept/courage,concept/honour,setting/oxford,character/clarence,character/duke_of_york,character/earl_of_warwick,character/hastings,character/henry_vi,character/richard"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter three Watchmen, to guard [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward’s tent |
| FIRST WATCHMAN | Come on, my masters, each man take his stand.<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] by this is set him down to [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]. |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | What, will he not to bed? |
| FIRST WATCHMAN | Why, no—for he hath made a solemn vow<br>Never to lie and take his natural rest<br>Till Warwick or himself be quite suppressed. |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | Tomorrow then belike shall be the day,<br>If Warwick be so near as men report. |
| THIRD WATCHMAN | But say, I pray, what nobleman is that<br>That with the [[archetypes/king\|King]] here resteth in his tent? |
| FIRST WATCHMAN | ’Tis the Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s chiefest friend. |
| THIRD WATCHMAN | O, is it so ? But why commands the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>That his chief followers lodge in towns about him,<br>While he himself keeps in the cold field? |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | ’Tis the more [[concepts/honour\|honour]], because more dangerous. |
| THIRD WATCHMAN | Ay, but give me worship and quietness—<br>I like it better than a dangerous [[concepts/honour\|honour]].<br>If Warwick knew in what estate he stands,<br>’Tis to be doubted he would waken him. |
| FIRST WATCHMAN | Unless our halberds did shut up his passage. |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | Ay, wherefore else guard we his royal tent<br>But to defend his person from [[motifs/night\|night]]-foes? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter silently the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]], George Duke of |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], and the Duke of<br><br>Somerset, with French soldiers |
| WARWICK | This is his tent—and see where stand his guard.<br>[[concepts/courage\|Courage]], my masters—[[concepts/honour\|honour]] now or never!<br>But follow me, and Edward shall be ours. |
| FIRST WATCHMAN | Who goes there? |
| SECOND WATCHMAN | Stay or thou diest.<br>Warwick and the rest all cry ‘Warwick, Warwick!’<br><br>and set upon the guard, who fly, crying ‘Arm, arm!’<br><br>Warwick and the rest follow them |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano tre Sentinelle, a guardia della tenda di [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo |
| PRIMA SENTINELLA | Avanti, padroni miei, ognuno prenda il suo posto.<br>Il [[archetypes/king\|re]] a quest’ora s’è coricato a [[motifs/sleep\|dormire]]. |
| SECONDA SENTINELLA | Che, non andrà a letto? |
| PRIMA SENTINELLA | Ebbene, no—ché ha fatto solenne voto<br>di non coricarsi mai né prendere il suo naturale riposo<br>finché Warwick o egli stesso non sia del tutto abbattuto. |
| SECONDA SENTINELLA | Domani, allora, sarà probabilmente il giorno,<br>se Warwick è così vicino come si dice. |
| TERZA SENTINELLA | Ma dite, vi prego, chi è quel gentiluomo<br>che qui riposa col [[archetypes/king\|re]] nella sua tenda? |
| PRIMA SENTINELLA | È lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], il maggiore amico del [[archetypes/king\|re]]. |
| TERZA SENTINELLA | Oh, è così ? Ma perché comanda il [[archetypes/king\|re]]<br>che i suoi principali seguaci alloggino nelle città intorno a lui,<br>mentre egli stesso si tiene nel freddo campo? |
| SECONDA SENTINELLA | È tanto più [[concepts/honour\|onore]], perché più pericoloso. |
| TERZA SENTINELLA | Sì, ma a me date onore e quiete—<br>li preferisco a un [[concepts/honour\|onore]] pericoloso.<br>Se Warwick sapesse in quale stato egli si trova,<br>c’è da dubitare che lo sveglierebbe. |
| PRIMA SENTINELLA | A meno che le nostre alabarde non gli chiudessero il passo. |
| SECONDA SENTINELLA | Sì, e perché altrimenti custodiamo la sua regale tenda<br>se non per difendere la sua persona dai nemici della [[motifs/night\|notte]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano silenziosamente il [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|conte di Warwick]], Giorgio duca di |
| SECONDA SENTINELLA | [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], il conte di [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], e il duca di<br><br>Somerset, con soldati francesi |
| WARWICK | Questa è la sua tenda—e guardate dove sta la sua guardia.<br>[[concepts/courage\|Coraggio]], padroni miei—[[concepts/honour\|onore]] ora o mai più!<br>Seguitemi soltanto, ed Edoardo sarà nostro. |
| PRIMA SENTINELLA | Chi va là? |
| SECONDA SENTINELLA | Fermo o muori.<br>Warwick e gli altri gridano tutti ‘Warwick, Warwick!’<br><br>e assalgono la guardia, che fugge, gridando ‘All’armi, all’armi!’<br><br>Warwick e gli altri li seguono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_4" data-title="Richard Duke of York (3 Henry VI) — Act IV, Scene 4" data-chapter="Scene 4" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,archetype/shadow,motif/crown,motif/wheel,motif/wind,concept/imprisonment,setting/london,setting/oxford,character/clarence,character/duke_of_gloucester,character/duke_of_york,character/earl_of_warwick,character/hastings,character/henry_vi,character/richard"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | With the drummer playing and trumpeter sounding, enter the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]], the Duke of Somerset, and the rest bringing [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward out in his gown, sitting in a chair. [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]] and Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] flies over the stage |
| SOMERSET | What are they that fly there? |
| WARWICK | [[characters/richard\|Richard]] and [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]]—let them go. Here is the Duke. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | ‘The Duke’! Why, Warwick, when we parted,<br>Thou calledst me [[archetypes/king\|king]]. |
| WARWICK | Ay, but the case is altered.<br>When you disgraced me in my embassade,<br>Then I degraded you from being [[archetypes/king\|king]],<br>And come now to create you [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]].<br>Alas, how should you govern any kingdom<br>That know not how to use ambassadors,<br>Nor how to be contented with one wife,<br>Nor how to use your brothers brotherly,<br>Nor how to study for the people’s welfare,<br>Nor how to shroud yourself from enemies? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | (seeing George)<br>Yea, brother of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], art thou here too?<br>Nay, then, I see that Edward needs must down.<br>Yet, Warwick, in despite of all mischance,<br>Of thee thyself and all thy complices,<br>Edward will always bear himself as [[archetypes/king\|king]].<br>Though fortune’s malice overthrow my state,<br>My mind exceeds the compass of her [[motifs/wheel\|wheel]]. |
| WARWICK | Then, for his mind, be Edward England’s [[archetypes/king\|king]].<br>Warwick takes off Edward’s [[motifs/crown\|crown]]<br>But Henry now shall wear the English [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>And be true [[archetypes/king\|king]] indeed, thou but the [[archetypes/shadow\|shadow]].<br>My lord of Somerset, at my request,<br>See that, forthwith, Duke Edward be conveyed<br>Unto my brother, Archbishop of York.<br>When I have fought with Pembroke and his fellows,<br>I’ll follow you, and tell what answer<br>Louis and the Lady Bona send to him.<br>Now for a while farewell, good [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]].<br>They begin to lead Edward out forcibly |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | What fates impose, that men must needs abide.<br>It boots not to resist both [[motifs/wind\|wind]] and tide. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some with Edward |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | What now remains, my lords, for us to do<br>But march to [[settings/london\|London]] with our soldiers? |
| WARWICK | Ay, that’s the first thing that we have to do—<br>To free [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry from [[concepts/imprisonment\|imprisonment]]<br>And see him seated in the regal throne. Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Col tamburino che suona e il trombettiere che squilla, entrano il [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|conte di Warwick]], il duca di Somerset, e gli altri, portando fuori [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo in veste da camera, seduto su una sedia. [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|duca di Gloucester]] e lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] fuggono attraverso la scena |
| SOMERSET | Chi sono quelli che fuggono là? |
| WARWICK | [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] e [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]]—lasciateli andare. Ecco il duca. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | ‘Il duca’! Ebbene, Warwick, quando ci separammo,<br>tu mi chiamavi [[archetypes/king\|re]]. |
| WARWICK | Sì, ma il caso è mutato.<br>Quando mi svergognaste nella mia ambasciata,<br>allora io vi degradai dall’esser [[archetypes/king\|re]],<br>e vengo ora a crearvi [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]].<br>Ahimè, come potreste governare un qualsiasi regno,<br>voi che non sapete come usare gli ambasciatori,<br>né come contentarvi d’una sola moglie,<br>né come trattare fraternamente i vostri fratelli,<br>né come studiare il bene del popolo,<br>né come guardarvi dai nemici? |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | (vedendo Giorgio)<br>Ah, fratello di [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], sei anche tu qui?<br>Ebbene, allora vedo che Edoardo deve cadere.<br>Tuttavia, Warwick, a dispetto d’ogni sventura,<br>di te stesso e di tutti i tuoi complici,<br>Edoardo si porterà sempre come [[archetypes/king\|re]].<br>Sebbene la malizia della fortuna abbatta il mio stato,<br>il mio animo eccede il giro della sua [[motifs/wheel\|ruota]]. |
| WARWICK | Allora, per il suo animo, sia Edoardo [[archetypes/king\|re]] d’Inghilterra.<br>Warwick toglie la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] a Edoardo<br>Ma Enrico ora porterà la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] inglese,<br>e sarà vero [[archetypes/king\|re]] in effetti, tu soltanto l’[[archetypes/shadow\|ombra]].<br>Mio signore di Somerset, a mia richiesta,<br>fate che, senza indugio, il duca Edoardo sia condotto<br>presso mio fratello, arcivescovo di York.<br>Quando avrò combattuto con Pembroke e i suoi compagni,<br>vi seguirò, e dirò quale risposta<br>Luigi e madama Bona gli mandano.<br>Ora per un poco addio, buon [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]].<br>Cominciano a condurre fuori Edoardo con la forza |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Ciò che i fati impongono, gli uomini devono sopportarlo.<br>Non giova resistere a [[motifs/wind\|vento]] e marea insieme. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono alcuni con Edoardo |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | Che resta ora, signori, da fare a noi<br>se non marciare su [[settings/london\|Londra]] coi nostri soldati? |
| WARWICK | Sì, è la prima cosa che abbiamo da fare—<br>liberare [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico dalla [[concepts/imprisonment\|prigionia]]<br>e vederlo insediato sul regale trono. Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_5" data-title="Richard Duke of York (3 Henry VI) — Act IV, Scene 5" data-chapter="Scene 5" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,archetype/tyrant,motif/blood,motif/cross,motif/crown,motif/decay,motif/love,motif/tears,concept/despair,concept/faith,concept/hope,setting/london,character/duke_of_york,character/henry_vi,character/queen,character/richard"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Earl Rivers and his sister, Lady Gray, Edward’s [[characters/queen\|queen]] |
| RIVERS | Madam, what makes you in this sudden change? |
| LADY GRAY | Why, brother Rivers, are you yet to learn<br>What late misfortune is befall’n [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward? |
| RIVERS | What? Loss of some pitched battle against Warwick? |
| LADY GRAY | No, but the loss of his own royal person. |
| RIVERS | Then is my sovereign slain? |
| LADY GRAY | Ay, almost stain—for he is taken prisoner,<br>Either betrayed by falsehood of his guard<br>Or by his foe surprised at unawares,<br>And, as I further have to understand,<br>Is new committed to the Bishop of York,<br>Fell Warwick’s brother, and by that our foe. |
| RIVERS | These news, I must confess, are full of grief.<br>Yet, gracious madam, bear it as you may.<br>Warwick may lose, that now hath won the day. |
| LADY GRAY | Till then fair [[concepts/hope\|hope]] must hinder life’s [[motifs/decay\|decay]],<br>And I the rather wean me from [[concepts/despair\|despair]]<br>For [[motifs/love\|love]] of Edward’s offspring in my womb.<br>This is it that makes me bridle passion<br>And bear with mildness my misfortune’s [[motifs/cross\|cross]].<br>Ay, ay, for this I draw in many a tear<br>And stop the rising of [[motifs/blood\|blood]]-sucking sighs,<br>Lest with my sighs or [[motifs/tears\|tears]] I blast or drown<br>[[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward’s fruit, true heir to th’English [[motifs/crown\|crown]]. |
| RIVERS | But, madam, where is Warwick then become? |
| LADY GRAY | I am informed that he comes towards [[settings/london\|London]]<br>To set the [[motifs/crown\|crown]] once more on Henry’s head.<br>Guess thou the rest—[[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward’s friends must down.<br>But to prevent the [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]]’s violence—<br>For trust not him that hath once broken [[concepts/faith\|faith]]—<br>I’ll hence forthwith unto the sanctuary,<br>To save at least the heir of Edward’s right.<br>There shall I rest secure from force and fraud.<br>Come, therefore, let us fly while we may fly.<br>If Warwick take us, we are sure to die. Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano il conte Rivers e sua sorella, madama Gray, [[characters/queen\|regina]] d’Edoardo |
| RIVERS | Signora, che vi fa così d’improvviso mutata? |
| MADAMA GRAY | Ebbene, fratello Rivers, dovete ancora apprendere<br>quale recente sventura è caduta su [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo? |
| RIVERS | Che? La perdita di qualche battaglia campale contro Warwick? |
| MADAMA GRAY | No, ma la perdita della sua stessa regale persona. |
| RIVERS | Allora il mio sovrano è ucciso? |
| MADAMA GRAY | Sì, quasi macchiato—ché è preso prigioniero,<br>o tradito dalla falsità della sua guardia<br>o sorpreso alla sprovvista dal suo nemico,<br>e, per quanto ho potuto oltre capire,<br>è ora affidato al vescovo di York,<br>fratello del feroce Warwick, e per ciò nostro nemico. |
| RIVERS | Queste nuove, devo confessarlo, sono piene di dolore.<br>Tuttavia, graziosa signora, sopportatelo come potete.<br>Warwick può perdere, lui che ora ha vinto la giornata. |
| MADAMA GRAY | Fino allora la bella [[concepts/hope\|speranza]] deve impedire il [[motifs/decay\|disfacimento]] della vita,<br>e tanto più mi svezzo dalla [[concepts/despair\|disperazione]]<br>per [[motifs/love\|amore]] della prole d’Edoardo nel mio ventre.<br>È questo che mi fa frenare la passione<br>e portare con mitezza la [[motifs/cross\|croce]] della mia sventura.<br>Sì, sì, per questo ricaccio dentro più d’una lacrima<br>e arresto il salire di sospiri che succhiano il [[motifs/blood\|sangue]],<br>perché coi miei sospiri o con le mie [[motifs/tears\|lacrime]] non arda o non anneghi<br>il frutto di [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo, vero erede della [[motifs/crown\|corona]] d’Inghilterra. |
| RIVERS | Ma, signora, dov’è Warwick andato allora? |
| MADAMA GRAY | Sono informata che viene verso [[settings/london\|Londra]]<br>per porre ancora una volta la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] sul capo di Enrico.<br>Indovina il resto—gli amici di [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo devono cadere.<br>Ma per prevenire la violenza del [[archetypes/tyrant\|tiranno]]—<br>ché non fidarti di chi ha già una volta rotto la [[concepts/faith\|fede]]—<br>me n’andrò subito di qui al santuario,<br>per salvare almeno l’erede del diritto d’Edoardo.<br>Là starò al sicuro da forza e da frode.<br>Vieni, dunque, fuggiamo mentre possiamo fuggire.<br>Se Warwick ci prende, siamo certi di morire. Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_6" data-title="Richard Duke of York (3 Henry VI) — Act IV, Scene 6" data-chapter="Scene 6" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,motif/crown,motif/horse,motif/time,motif/well,concept/wonder,character/duke_of_gloucester,character/duke_of_york,character/gloucester,character/hastings,character/henry_vi,character/richard"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], and Sir William Stanley, with soldiers |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now my lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] and Sir William Stanley,<br>Leave off to [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] why I drew you hither<br>Into this chiefest thicket of the park.<br>Thus stands the case: you know our [[archetypes/king\|King]], my brother,<br>Is prisoner to the Bishop here, at whose hands<br>He hath good usage and great liberty,<br>And, often but attended with weak guard,<br>Comes hunting this way to disport himself.<br>I have advertised him by secret means<br>That if about this hour he make this way<br>Under the colour of his usual game,<br>He shall here find his friends with [[motifs/horse\|horse]] and men<br>To set him free from his captivity. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward and a Huntsman with him |
| HUNTSMAN | This way, my lord—for this way lies the game. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Nay, this way, man—see where the huntsmen stand.<br>Now, brother of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]], Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], and the<br>rest,<br>Stand you thus close to steal the Bishop’s deer? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Brother, the [[motifs/time\|time]] and case requireth haste.<br>Your [[motifs/horse\|horse]] stands ready at the park corner. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | But whither shall we then? |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | To Lynn, my lord,<br>And shipped from thence to Flanders. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] aside |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]] guessed, believe me—for that was my meaning. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Stanley, I will requite thy forwardness. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | But wherefore stay we? ’Tis no [[motifs/time\|time]] to talk. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Huntsman, what sayst thou? Wilt thou go along? |
| HUNTSMAN | Better do so than tarry and be hanged. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Come then, away—let’s have no more ado. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Bishop, farewell—shield thee from Warwick’s frown,<br>And pray that I may repossess the [[motifs/crown\|crown]]. Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|duca di Gloucester]], lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], e sir William Stanley, con soldati |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ora, mio lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] e sir William Stanley,<br>cessate di [[concepts/wonder\|meravigliarvi]] perché v’ho condotti qui<br>in questo folto più profondo del parco.<br>Il caso sta così: sapete che il nostro [[archetypes/king\|re]], mio fratello,<br>è prigioniero del vescovo qui, dalle cui mani<br>riceve buon trattamento e grande libertà,<br>e, spesso accompagnato da debole scorta,<br>viene a caccia per questa via a diportarsi.<br>L’ho avvertito per segrete vie<br>che se verso quest’ora prende questa strada<br>sotto il pretesto della sua caccia usuale,<br>troverà qui i suoi amici con [[motifs/horse\|cavalli]] e uomini<br>per liberarlo dalla sua cattività. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo e con lui un Cacciatore |
| CACCIATORE | Per qui, mio signore—ché per qui sta la selvaggina. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | No, per qui, uomo—guarda dove stanno i cacciatori.<br>Ora, fratello di [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]], lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], e voi<br>altri,<br>state così appostati per rubare i cervi del vescovo? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Fratello, il [[motifs/time\|tempo]] e il caso richiedono fretta.<br>Il vostro [[motifs/horse\|cavallo]] è pronto all’angolo del parco. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Ma dove andremo poi? |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | A Lynn, mio signore,<br>e di là imbarcati per le Fiandre. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] a parte |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | [[motifs/well\|Bene]] indovinato, credetemi—ché era proprio il mio pensiero. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Stanley, ricompenserò la tua premura. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ma perché indugiamo? Non è [[motifs/time\|tempo]] di parlare. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Cacciatore, che dici tu? Vuoi venire con noi? |
| CACCIATORE | Meglio farlo che restare ed essere impiccato. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Vieni allora, via—non facciamo più indugi. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Vescovo, addio—guàrdati dal cipiglio di Warwick,<br>e prega ch’io possa riavere la [[motifs/crown\|corona]]. Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_7" data-title="Richard Duke of York (3 Henry VI) — Act IV, Scene 7" data-chapter="Scene 7" data-kind="scene" data-tags="topos/double,archetype/king,archetype/shadow,motif/birds,motif/crown,motif/nature,motif/prayer,motif/stars,motif/time,motif/well,concept/body,concept/fear,concept/honour,concept/hope,concept/nature,concept/truth,setting/oxford,character/clarence,character/duke_of_gloucester,character/duke_of_york,character/earl_of_richmond,character/earl_of_warwick,character/hastings,character/henry_vi,character/queen,character/richard,character/speed"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]] and George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] with the [[motifs/crown\|crown]]. Then enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry, the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], the Duke of Somerset withl young Henry Ear of Richmond, the Marquis of Montague, and the Lieutenant of the Tower |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Master Lieutenant, now that God and friends<br>Have shaken Edward from the regal seat<br>And turned my captive state to liberty,<br>My [[concepts/fear\|fear]] to [[concepts/hope\|hope]], my sorrows unto joys,<br>At our enlargement what are thy due fees? |
| LIEUTENANT | Subjects may challenge nothing of their sovereigns—<br>But if an humble [[motifs/prayer\|prayer]] may prevail,<br>I then crave pardon of your majesty. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | For what, Lieutenant? For [[motifs/well\|well]] using me?<br>Nay, be thou sure I’ll [[motifs/well\|well]] requite thy kindness,<br>For that it made my prisonment a pleasure—<br>Ay, such a pleasure as encagèd [[motifs/birds\|birds]]<br>Conceive when, after many moody thoughts,<br>At last by notes of household harmony<br>They quite forget their loss of liberty.<br>But, Warwick, after God, thou sett’st me free,<br>And chiefly therefore I thank God and thee.<br>He was the author, thou the instrument.<br>Therefore, that I may conquer fortune’s spite<br>By living low, where fortune cannot hurt me,<br>And that the people of this blessed land<br>May not be punished with my thwarting [[motifs/stars\|stars]],<br>Warwick, although my head still wear the [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>I here resign my government to thee,<br>For thou art fortunate in all thy deeds. |
| WARWICK | Your grace hath still been famed for virtuous,<br>And now may seem as wise as virtuous<br>By spying and avoiding fortune’s malice,<br>For few men rightly temper with the [[motifs/stars\|stars]].<br>Yet in this one thing let me blame your grace:<br>For choosing me when [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] is in place. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | No, Warwick, thou art worthy of the sway,<br>To whom the heav’ns in thy nativity<br>Adjudged an olive branch and laurel [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>As likely to be blest in peace and war.<br>And therefore I yield thee my free consent. |
| WARWICK | And I choose [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] only for Protector. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Warwick and [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], give me both your hands.<br>Now join your hands, and with your hands your<br>hearts,<br>That no dissension hinder government.<br>I make you both Protectors of this land,<br>While I myself will lead a private life<br>And in devotion spend my latter days,<br>To sin’s rebuke and my creator’s praise. |
| WARWICK | What answers [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] to his sovereign’s will? |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | That he consents, if Warwick yield consent,<br>For on thy fortune I repose myself. |
| WARWICK | Why, then, though loath, yet must I be content.<br>We’ll yoke together, like a [[topoi/double\|double]] [[archetypes/shadow\|shadow]]<br>To Henry’s [[concepts/body\|body]], and supply his place—<br>I mean in bearing weight of government—<br>While he enjoys the [[concepts/honour\|honour]] and his ease.<br>And, [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], now then it is more than needful<br>Forthwith that Edward be pronounced a traitor,<br>And all his lands and goods be confiscate. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | What else? And that succession be determined. |
| WARWICK | Ay, therein [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] shall not want his part. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | But with the first of all your chief affairs,<br>Let me entreat—for I command no more—<br>That Margaret your [[characters/queen\|queen]] and my son Edward<br>Be sent for, to return from France with [[characters/speed\|speed]].<br>For, till I see them here, by doubtful [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>My joy of liberty is half eclipsed. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | It shall be done, my sovereign, with all [[characters/speed\|speed]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | My lord of Somerset, what youth is that<br>Of whom you seem to have so tender care? |
| SOMERSET | My liege, it is young Henry, [[characters/earl-of-richmond\|Earl of Richmond]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Come hither, England’s [[concepts/hope\|hope]].<br>[[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry lays his hand on Richmond’s head If secret powers<br>Suggest but [[concepts/truth\|truth]] to my divining thoughts,<br>This pretty lad will prove our country’s bliss.<br>His looks are full of peaceful majesty,<br>His head by [[concepts/nature\|nature]] framed to wear a [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>His hand to wield a sceptre, and himself<br>Likely in [[motifs/time\|time]] to bless a regal throne.<br>Make much of him, my lords, for this is he<br>Must help you more than you are hurt by me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Post |
| WARWICK | What news, my friend? |
| POST | That Edward is escaped from your brother<br>And fled, as he hears since, to Burgundy. |
| WARWICK | Unsavoury news—but how made he escape ? |
| POST | He was conveyed by [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]]<br>And the Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], who attended him<br>In secret ambush on the forest side<br>And from the Bishop’s huntsmen rescued him—<br>For hunting was his daily exercise. |
| WARWICK | My brother was too careless of his charge.<br>(To [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry) But let us hence, my sovereign, to<br>provide<br>A salve for any sore that may betide. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but Somerset, Richmond, and [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] |
| SOMERSET | (to [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]])<br>My lord, I like not of this flight of Edward’s,<br>For doubtless Burgundy will yield him help,<br>And we shall have more wars before’t be long.<br>As Henry’s late presaging prophecy<br>Did glad my heart with [[concepts/hope\|hope]] of this young Richmond,<br>So doth my heart misgive me, in these conflicts,<br>What may befall him, to his harm and ours.<br>Therefore, Lord [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], to prevent the worst,<br>Forthwith we’ll send him hence to Brittany,<br>Till storms be past of civil enmity. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | Ay, for if Edward repossess the [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>’Tis like that Richmond with the rest shall down. |
| SOMERSET | It shall be so—he shall to Brittany.<br>Come, therefore, let’s about it speedily. Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Squilli. Entrano il [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|conte di Warwick]] e Giorgio duca di [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] con la [[motifs/crown\|corona]]. Poi entrano [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico, il conte di [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], il duca di Somerset col giovane Enrico conte di Richmond, il marchese di Montague, e il Luogotenente della Torre |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Signor Luogotenente, ora che Dio e gli amici<br>hanno scrollato Edoardo dal seggio regale<br>e volto il mio stato di prigioniero in libertà,<br>il mio [[concepts/fear\|timore]] in [[concepts/hope\|speranza]], i miei dolori in gioie,<br>alla nostra liberazione quali sono le vostre spettanze? |
| LUOGOTENENTE | I sudditi non possono esigere nulla dai loro sovrani—<br>ma se un’umile [[motifs/prayer\|preghiera]] può prevalere,<br>allora chiedo perdono a vostra maestà. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Di che, Luogotenente? Per avermi [[motifs/well\|ben]] trattato?<br>No, siate certo che ricompenserò [[motifs/well\|bene]] la vostra cortesia,<br>ché essa fece della mia prigionia un piacere—<br>sì, tal piacere quale concepiscono gli [[motifs/birds\|uccelli]] in gabbia<br>quando, dopo molti pensieri malinconici,<br>alfine, per note d’armonia domestica,<br>dimenticano affatto la loro perduta libertà.<br>Ma, Warwick, dopo Dio, tu mi liberasti,<br>e principalmente perciò ringrazio Dio e te.<br>Egli fu l’autore, tu lo strumento.<br>Perciò, affinché io vinca il dispetto della fortuna<br>vivendo in basso, dove la fortuna non può ferirmi,<br>e affinché il popolo di questa terra benedetta<br>non sia punito per le mie [[motifs/stars\|stelle]] avverse,<br>Warwick, sebbene il mio capo porti ancora la [[motifs/crown\|corona]],<br>io qui rassegno a te il mio governo,<br>ché tu sei fortunato in tutte le tue azioni. |
| WARWICK | Vostra grazia è sempre stata famosa per virtù,<br>e ora può sembrare tanto saggia quanto virtuosa<br>nello scorgere ed evitare la malizia della fortuna,<br>ché pochi uomini si temperano rettamente con le [[motifs/stars\|stelle]].<br>Tuttavia in questa sola cosa lasciate ch’io biasimi vostra grazia:<br>d’aver scelto me quando [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] è presente. |
| GIORGIO DI [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | No, Warwick, tu sei degno del potere,<br>tu a cui i cieli nella tua nascita<br>assegnarono un ramo d’olivo e una [[motifs/crown\|corona]] di lauro,<br>come destinato a esser benedetto in pace e in guerra.<br>E perciò io ti do il mio libero consenso. |
| WARWICK | E io scelgo [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] come solo Protettore. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Warwick e [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], datemi entrambi le vostre mani.<br>Ora unite le vostre mani, e con le mani i vostri<br>cuori,<br>sì che nessuna discordia impedisca il governo.<br>Vi faccio entrambi Protettori di questa terra,<br>mentre io stesso condurrò vita privata<br>e in devozione spenderò i miei ultimi giorni,<br>a rimprovero del peccato e a lode del mio creatore. |
| WARWICK | Che risponde [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] alla volontà del suo sovrano? |
| GIORGIO DI [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Che consente, se Warwick dà consenso,<br>ché sulla tua fortuna io mi riposo. |
| WARWICK | Ebbene, allora, sebbene a contraggenio, devo essere contento.<br>Ci aggiogheremo insieme, come [[topoi/double\|doppia]] [[archetypes/shadow\|ombra]]<br>al [[concepts/body\|corpo]] di Enrico, e ne terremo il luogo—<br>intendo nel portare il peso del governo—<br>mentre egli goda l’[[concepts/honour\|onore]] e il suo agio.<br>E, [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], ora è più che necessario<br>che senza indugio Edoardo sia dichiarato traditore,<br>e tutte le sue terre e i suoi beni confiscati. |
| GIORGIO DI [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Che altro? E che la successione sia stabilita. |
| WARWICK | Sì, e in questo [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] non mancherà della sua parte. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Ma fra i primi di tutti i vostri principali affari,<br>lasciate ch’io supplichi—ché non comando più—<br>che Margherita vostra [[characters/queen\|regina]] e mio figlio Edoardo<br>siano mandati a chiamare, per tornare dalla Francia in [[characters/speed\|fretta]].<br>Ché, finché non li vedo qui, per dubbioso [[concepts/fear\|timore]]<br>la mia gioia della libertà è mezzo eclissata. |
| GIORGIO DI [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Sarà fatto, mio sovrano, con ogni [[characters/speed\|prestezza]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Mio signore di Somerset, chi è quel giovane<br>di cui sembrate avere così tenera cura? |
| SOMERSET | Mio sire, è il giovane Enrico, [[characters/earl-of-richmond\|conte di Richmond]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Vieni qua, [[concepts/hope\|speranza]] d’Inghilterra.<br>[[archetypes/king\|Re]] Enrico posa la mano sul capo di Richmond Se potenze segrete<br>suggeriscono [[concepts/truth\|verità]] ai miei pensieri divinatori,<br>questo bel fanciullo sarà la felicità del nostro paese.<br>Il suo aspetto è pieno di pacifica maestà,<br>il suo capo dalla [[concepts/nature\|natura]] foggiato a portare una [[motifs/crown\|corona]],<br>la sua mano a reggere uno scettro, ed egli stesso<br>destinato col [[motifs/time\|tempo]] a benedire un trono regale.<br>Tenetelo caro, signori, ché questi è colui<br>che dovrà aiutarvi più di quanto io v’abbia nuociuto. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra un Corriere |
| WARWICK | Che nuove, amico mio? |
| CORRIERE | Che Edoardo è fuggito da vostro fratello<br>e scappato, come poi ha udito, in Borgogna. |
| WARWICK | Nuove ingrate—ma come fece a fuggire ? |
| CORRIERE | Fu condotto via da [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|duca di Gloucester]]<br>e da lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], che l’attendevano<br>in segreto agguato al margine della foresta<br>e lo strapparono ai cacciatori del vescovo—<br>ché la caccia era il suo esercizio quotidiano. |
| WARWICK | Mio fratello fu troppo negligente della sua custodia.<br>(A [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico) Ma andiamo di qui, mio sovrano, a<br>provvedere<br>un unguento per qualunque piaga possa sopravvenire. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono tutti tranne Somerset, Richmond e [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] |
| SOMERSET | (a [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]])<br>Mio signore, non mi piace questa fuga d’Edoardo,<br>ché senza dubbio la Borgogna gli darà aiuto,<br>e avremo altre guerre prima che passi molto.<br>Come la profezia presaga poc’anzi di Enrico<br>rallegrò il mio cuore con la [[concepts/hope\|speranza]] di questo giovane Richmond,<br>così il mio cuore mi presagisce male, in questi conflitti,<br>di ciò che può accadergli, a suo danno e nostro.<br>Perciò, lord [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], per prevenire il peggio,<br>senza indugio lo manderemo di qui in Bretagna,<br>finché non siano passate le tempeste dell’inimicizia civile. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | Sì, ché se Edoardo riprende la [[motifs/crown\|corona]],<br>è probabile che Richmond con gli altri cada. |
| SOMERSET | Sarà così—andrà in Bretagna.<br>Venite, dunque, mettiamoci all’opera in fretta. Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_8" data-title="Richard Duke of York (3 Henry VI) — Act IV, Scene 8" data-chapter="Scene 8" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,motif/crown,motif/night,motif/storm,motif/time,motif/well,concept/body,concept/doubt,concept/fear,character/clarence,character/duke_of_gloucester,character/duke_of_york,character/gloucester,character/hastings,character/henry_vi,character/richard"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward, [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], and Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], With a troop of Hollanders |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Now, brother [[characters/richard\|Richard]], Lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], and the rest,<br>Yet thus far fortune maketh us amends,<br>And says that once more I shall interchange<br>My waned state for Henry’s regal [[motifs/crown\|crown]].<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]] have we passed and now repassed the seas<br>And brought desired help from Burgundy.<br>What then remains, we being thus arrived<br>From Ravenspurgh haven before the gates of York,<br>But that we enter, as into our dukedom ? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] knocks at the gates of York |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The gates made fast? Brother, I like not this.<br>For many men that stumble at the threshold<br>Are [[motifs/well\|well]] foretold that danger lurks within. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Tush, man, abodements must not now affright us.<br>By fair or foul means we must enter in,<br>For hither will our friends repair to us. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | My liege, I’ll knock once more to summon them.<br>He knocks. Enter, on the walls, the Mayor and aldermen of York |
| MAYOR | My lords, we were forewarned of your coming,<br>And shut the gates for safety of oursetves—<br>For now we owe allegiance unto Henry. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | But, Master Mayor, if Henry be your [[archetypes/king\|king]],<br>Yet Edward at the least is [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]]. |
| MAYOR | True, my good lord, I know you for no less. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Why, and I challenge nothing but my dukedom,<br>As being [[motifs/well\|well]] content with that alone. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (aside)<br>But when the fox hath once got in his nose,<br>He’ll soon find means to make the [[concepts/body\|body]] follow. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Why, Master Mayor, why stand you in a [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]?<br>Open the gates—we are [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry’s friends. |
| MAYOR | Ay, say you so? The gates shall then be opened.<br>They descend |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | A wise stout captain, and soon persuaded. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | The good old man would fain that all were [[motifs/well\|well]],<br>So ’twere not long of him; but being entered,<br>I [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] not, I, but we shall soon persuade<br>Both him and all his brothers unto reason. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter below the Mayor and two aldermen |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | So, Master Mayor, these gates must not be shut<br>But in the [[motifs/night\|night]] or in the [[motifs/time\|time]] of war.<br>What—[[concepts/fear\|fear]] not, man, but yield me up the keys,<br>[[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward takes some keys from the Mayor<br>For Edward will defend the town and thee,<br>And all those friends that deign to follow me.<br>March. Enter Sir John Montgomery with a drummer and soldiers |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Brother, this is Sir John Montgomery,<br>Our trusty friend, unless I be deceived. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Welcome, Sir John—but why come you in arms? |
| MONTGOMERY | To help [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward in his [[motifs/time\|time]] of [[motifs/storm\|storm]],<br>As every loyal subject ought to do. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Thanks, good Montgomery, but we now forget<br>Our title to the [[motifs/crown\|crown]], and only claim<br>Our dukedom till God please to send the rest. |
| MONTGOMERY | Then fare you [[motifs/well\|well]], for I will hence again.<br>I came to serve a [[archetypes/king\|king]] and not a duke.<br>Drummer, strike up, and let us march away. 50<br>The drummer begins to sound a march |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Nay, stay, Sir John, a while, and we’ll debate<br>By what safe means the [[motifs/crown\|crown]] may be recovered. |
| MONTGOMERY | What talk you of debating? In few words,<br>If you’ll not here proclaim yourself our [[archetypes/king\|king]]<br>I’ll leave you to your fortune and be gone<br>To keep them back that come to succour you.<br>Why shall we fight, if you pretend no title? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward)<br>Why, brother, wherefore stand you on nice points? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | When we grow stronger, then we’ll make our claim.<br>Till then ’tis wisdom to conceal our meaning. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Away with scrupulous wit! Now arms must rule. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And fearless minds climb soonest unto crowns.<br>Brother, we will proclaim you out of hand,<br>The bruit thereof will bring you many friends. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Then be it as you will, for ’tis my right,<br>And Henry but usurps the diadem. |
| MONTGOMERY | Ay, now my sovereign speaketh like himself,<br>And now will I be Edward’s champion. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound trumpet, Edward shall be here proclaimed. |
| *(didascalia)* | To Montgomery |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Come, fellow soldier, make thou proclamation. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish |
| *(didascalia)* | MONTGOMERY Edward the Fourth, by the grace of God |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | [[archetypes/king\|King]] of England and France, and Lord of Ireland—<br>And whosoe’er gainsays [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward’s right,<br>By this I challenge him to single fight.<br>He throws down his gauntlet |
| ALL | Long live Edward the Fourth! |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Thanks, brave Montgomery, and thanks unto you all.<br>If fortune serve me I’ll requite this kindness.<br>Now, for this [[motifs/night\|night]], let’s harbour here in York;<br>And when the morning sun shall raise his car<br>Above the border of this horizon,<br>We’ll forward towards Warwick and his mates.<br>For [[motifs/well\|well]] I wot that Henry is no soldier.<br>Ah, froward [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], how evil it beseems thee<br>To flatter Henry and forsake thy brother!<br>Yet, as we may, we’ll meet both thee and Warwick.<br>Come on, brave soldiers—[[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] not of the day<br>And, that once gotten, [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] not of large pay. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Squilli. Entrano [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo, [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|duca di Gloucester]], e lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], con una schiera di Olandesi |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Ora, fratello [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]], lord [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]], e voi altri,<br>fin qui la fortuna ci risarcisce,<br>e dice che ancora una volta baratterò<br>il mio stato scemato con la regale [[motifs/crown\|corona]] di Enrico.<br>[[motifs/well\|Bene]] abbiamo varcato e ora rivarcato i mari<br>e portato il desiderato aiuto dalla Borgogna.<br>Che resta dunque, essendo così arrivati<br>dal porto di Ravenspurgh davanti alle porte di York,<br>se non che entriamo, come nel nostro ducato ? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/hastings\|Hastings]] batte alle porte di York |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Le porte serrate? Fratello, questo non mi piace.<br>Ché a molti uomini che inciampano sulla soglia<br>è [[motifs/well\|ben]] predetto che il pericolo s’annida dentro. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Bah, uomo, i presagi non devono ora spaventarci.<br>Con mezzi buoni o cattivi dobbiamo entrare,<br>ché qui i nostri amici verranno a raggiungerci. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Mio sire, batterò ancora una volta per chiamarli.<br>Batte. Entrano, sulle mura, il Sindaco e gli assessori di York |
| SINDACO | Signori, fummo avvertiti del vostro arrivo,<br>e chiudemmo le porte per la sicurezza di noi stessi—<br>ché ora dobbiamo fedeltà a Enrico. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Ma, signor Sindaco, se Enrico è il vostro [[archetypes/king\|re]],<br>Edoardo è almeno [[characters/duke-of-york\|duca di York]]. |
| SINDACO | Vero, mio buon signore, non vi conosco per meno. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Ebbene, e io non rivendico altro che il mio ducato,<br>essendo [[motifs/well\|ben]] contento di quello solo. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (a parte)<br>Ma quando la volpe ha una volta cacciato dentro il naso,<br>trova presto il modo di far seguire il [[concepts/body\|corpo]]. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Ebbene, signor Sindaco, perché state in [[concepts/doubt\|dubbio]]?<br>Aprite le porte—siamo amici di [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico. |
| SINDACO | Ah, dite così? Allora le porte saranno aperte.<br>Scendono |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Un capitano saggio e gagliardo, e presto persuaso. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Il buon vecchio vorrebbe che tutto andasse [[motifs/well\|bene]],<br>purché non dipendesse da lui; ma una volta entrati,<br>non [[concepts/doubt\|dubito]], io, che presto persuaderemo<br>lui e tutti i suoi confratelli alla ragione. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano in basso il Sindaco e due assessori |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Così, signor Sindaco, queste porte non devono esser chiuse<br>se non di [[motifs/night\|notte]] o in [[motifs/time\|tempo]] di guerra.<br>Che—non [[concepts/fear\|temete]], uomo, ma consegnatemi le chiavi,<br>[[archetypes/king\|Re]] Edoardo prende alcune chiavi dal Sindaco<br>ché Edoardo difenderà la città e voi,<br>e tutti quegli amici che si degnano di seguirmi.<br>Marcia. Entra sir John Montgomery con un tamburino e soldati |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Fratello, questi è sir John Montgomery,<br>nostro fidato amico, se non m’inganno. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Benvenuto, sir John—ma perché venite in armi? |
| MONTGOMERY | Per aiutare [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo nel suo [[motifs/time\|tempo]] di [[motifs/storm\|tempesta]],<br>come ogni leale suddito deve fare. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Grazie, buon Montgomery, ma noi ora dimentichiamo<br>il nostro titolo alla [[motifs/crown\|corona]], e reclamiamo soltanto<br>il nostro ducato, finché a Dio piaccia mandare il resto. |
| MONTGOMERY | Allora state [[motifs/well\|bene]], ché io tornerò indietro.<br>Venni a servire un [[archetypes/king\|re]] e non un duca.<br>Tamburino, batti, e marciamo via. 50<br>Il tamburino comincia a battere una marcia |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | No, restate, sir John, un poco, e discuteremo<br>con quali mezzi sicuri la [[motifs/crown\|corona]] possa esser riconquistata. |
| MONTGOMERY | Che parlate di discutere? In poche parole,<br>se non vi proclamate qui nostro [[archetypes/king\|re]],<br>vi lascerò alla vostra fortuna e me n’andrò<br>a trattenere quelli che vengono a soccorrervi.<br>Perché combatteremo, se non pretendete alcun titolo? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (a [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo)<br>Ebbene, fratello, perché insistete su sottigliezze? |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Quando cresceremo più forti, allora avanzeremo la nostra pretesa.<br>Fino allora è saggezza celare il nostro intento. |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Via l’ingegno scrupoloso! Ora devono governare le armi. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | E gli animi senza paura salgono più presto alle corone.<br>Fratello, vi proclameremo senza indugio,<br>la fama di ciò vi porterà molti amici. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Allora sia come volete, ché è mio diritto,<br>ed Enrico non fa che usurpare il diadema. |
| MONTGOMERY | Sì, ora il mio sovrano parla da sé stesso,<br>e ora io sarò il campione d’Edoardo. |
| *(didascalia)* | Suoni la tromba, Edoardo sarà qui proclamato. |
| *(didascalia)* | A Montgomery |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Vieni, compagno soldato, fa’ tu il proclama. |
| *(didascalia)* | Squilli |
| *(didascalia)* | MONTGOMERY Edoardo Quarto, per grazia di Dio |
| [[characters/hastings\|HASTINGS]] | [[archetypes/king\|re]] d’Inghilterra e di Francia, e signore d’Irlanda—<br>e chiunque contesti il diritto di [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo,<br>con questo lo sfido a singolo combattimento.<br>Getta a terra il suo guanto |
| TUTTI | Viva Edoardo Quarto! |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Grazie, bravo Montgomery, e grazie a voi tutti.<br>Se la fortuna mi serve, ricompenserò questa cortesia.<br>Ora, per questa [[motifs/night\|notte]], alberghiamo qui in York;<br>e quando il sole del mattino solleverà il suo carro<br>sopra il confine di questo orizzonte,<br>avanzeremo verso Warwick e i suoi compagni.<br>Ché [[motifs/well\|bene]] so che Enrico non è soldato.<br>Ah, ostinato [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], quanto male ti si addice<br>lusingare Enrico e abbandonare tuo fratello!<br>Tuttavia, come potremo, incontreremo te e Warwick.<br>Avanti, bravi soldati—non [[concepts/doubt\|dubitate]] della giornata,<br>e, ottenuta quella, non [[concepts/doubt\|dubitate]] di larga paga. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_9" data-title="Richard Duke of York (3 Henry VI) — Act IV, Scene 9" data-chapter="Scene 9" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,motif/fire,motif/lamb,motif/lion,motif/love,motif/spring,motif/tears,motif/water,motif/well,motif/winter,concept/doubt,concept/fear,concept/hope,concept/mercy,concept/revenge,concept/truth,setting/london,setting/oxford,character/buckingham,character/clarence,character/duke_of_exeter,character/duke_of_gloucester,character/duke_of_york,character/earl_of_warwick,character/gloucester,character/hector,character/henry_vi,character/kent,character/richard"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry, the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]], the Marquis of Montague, George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], and the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] |
| WARWICK | What counsel, lords? Edward from Belgia,<br>With hasty Germans and blunt Hollanders,<br>Hath passed in safety through the narrow seas,<br>And with his troops doth march amain to [[settings/london\|London]],<br>And many giddy people flock to him. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Let’s levy men and beat him back again. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | A little [[motifs/fire\|fire]] is quickly trodden out,<br>Which, being suffered, rivers cannot quench. |
| WARWICK | In Warwickshire I have true-hearted friends,<br>Not mutinous in peace, yet bold in war.<br>Those will I muster up. And thou, son [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]],<br>Shalt stir in Suffolk, Norfolk, and in [[characters/kent\|Kent]],<br>The knights and gentlemen to come with thee.<br>Thou, brother Montague, in [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]],<br>Northampton, and in Leicestershire shalt find<br>Men [[motifs/well\|well]] inclined to hear what thou command’st.<br>And thou, brave [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], wondrous [[motifs/well\|well]] beloved<br>In Oxfordshire, shalt muster up thy friends.<br>My sovereign, with the loving citizens,<br>Like to his island girt in with the ocean,<br>Or modest Dian circled with her nymphs,<br>Shall rest in [[settings/london\|London]] till we come to him.<br>Fair lords, take leave and stand not to reply.<br>Farewell, my sovereign. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Farewell, my [[characters/hector\|Hector]], and my Troy’s true [[concepts/hope\|hope]]. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | In sign of [[concepts/truth\|truth]], I kiss your highness’ hand.<br>He kisses [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry’s hand |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | [[motifs/well\|Well]]-minded [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], be thou fortunate. |
| MONTAGUE | Comfort, my lord, and so I take my leave. |
| *(didascalia)* | He kisses [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry’s hand |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | And thus I seal my [[concepts/truth\|truth]] and bid adieu. |
| *(didascalia)* | He kisses [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry’s hand |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Sweet [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], and my loving Montague,<br>And all at once, once more a happy farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| WARWICK | Farewell, sweet lords—let’s meet at Coventry. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally! |
| *(didascalia)* | 4.10 Erenter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry and the [[characters/duke-of-exeter\|Duke of Exeter]] |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Here at the palace will I rest a while.<br>Cousin of Exeter, what thinks your lordship?<br>Methinks the power that Edward hath in field<br>Should not be able to encounter mine. |
| EXETER | The [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] is that he will seduce the rest. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | That’s not my [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. My meed hath got me fame.<br>I have not stopped mine ears to their demands,<br>Nor posted off their suits with slow delays.<br>My pity hath been balm to heal their wounds,<br>My mildness hath allayed their swelling griefs,<br>My [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] dried their [[motifs/water\|water]]-flowing [[motifs/tears\|tears]].<br>I have not been desirous of their wealth,<br>Nor much oppressed them with great subsidies,<br>Nor forward of [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]], though they much erred.<br>Then why should they [[motifs/love\|love]] Edward more than me?<br>No, Exeter, these graces challenge grace;<br>And when the [[motifs/lion\|lion]] fawns upon the [[motifs/lamb\|lamb]],<br>The [[motifs/lamb\|lamb]] will never cease to follow him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Shout within ‘A Lancaster’, ‘A York’ |
| EXETER | Hark, hark, my ford—what shouts are these? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward and [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], with soldiers |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Seize on the shame-faced Henry—bear him hence,<br>And once again proclaim us [[archetypes/king\|King]] of England.<br>You are the fount that makes small brooks to flow.<br>Now stops thy [[motifs/spring\|spring]]—my sea shall suck them dry,<br>And swell so much the higher by their ebb.<br>Hence with him to the Tower—tet him not speak. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some with [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry and Exeter |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | And lords, towards Coventry bend we our course,<br>Where peremptory Warwick now remains.<br>The sun shines hot, and, if we use delay,<br>Cold biting [[motifs/winter\|winter]] mars our hoped-for hay. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Away betimes, before his forces join,<br>And take the great-grown traitor unawares.<br>Brave warriors, march amain towards Coventry. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Squilli. Entrano [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico, il [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|conte di Warwick]], il marchese di Montague, Giorgio duca di [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], e il conte di [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] |
| WARWICK | Che consiglio, signori? Edoardo dal Belgio,<br>con Tedeschi impetuosi e rozzi Olandesi,<br>è passato in salvo attraverso gli stretti mari,<br>e con le sue truppe marcia a tutta forza verso [[settings/london\|Londra]],<br>e molta gente volubile accorre a lui. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Arruoliamo uomini e ricacciamolo indietro. |
| GIORGIO DI [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Un piccolo [[motifs/fire\|fuoco]] si calpesta presto,<br>il quale, se tollerato, i fiumi non possono spegnere. |
| WARWICK | Nel Warwickshire ho amici dal cuore fedele,<br>non sediziosi in pace, e tuttavia audaci in guerra.<br>Quelli io radunerò. E tu, figlio [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]],<br>ti muoverai nel Suffolk, nel Norfolk e nel [[characters/kent\|Kent]],<br>perché i cavalieri e i gentiluomini vengano con te.<br>Tu, fratello Montague, nel [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]],<br>nel Northampton e nel Leicestershire troverai<br>uomini [[motifs/well\|ben]] disposti a udire ciò che comandi.<br>E tu, bravo [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], meravigliosamente [[motifs/well\|ben]] amato<br>nell’Oxfordshire, radunerai i tuoi amici.<br>Il mio sovrano, coi cittadini affezionati,<br>come la sua isola cinta dall’oceano,<br>o la modesta Diana circondata dalle sue ninfe,<br>riposerà a [[settings/london\|Londra]] finché non veniamo a lui.<br>Bei signori, prendete congedo e non state a replicare.<br>Addio, mio sovrano. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Addio, mio [[characters/hector\|Ettore]], e vera [[concepts/hope\|speranza]] della mia Troia. |
| GIORGIO DI [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | In segno di [[concepts/truth\|fedeltà]], bacio la mano di vostra altezza.<br>Bacia la mano di [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | [[motifs/well\|Ben]] intenzionato [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], sii tu fortunato. |
| MONTAGUE | Confortatevi, mio signore, e così prendo congedo. |
| *(didascalia)* | Bacia la mano di [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | E così sigillo la mia [[concepts/truth\|fedeltà]] e dico addio. |
| *(didascalia)* | Bacia la mano di [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Dolce [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], e mio amato Montague,<br>e tutti insieme, ancora una volta un felice addio. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| WARWICK | Addio, dolci signori—incontriamoci a Coventry. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono separatamente! |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_10" data-title="Richard Duke of York (3 Henry VI) — Act IV, Scene 10" data-chapter="Scene 10" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,motif/lamb,motif/lion,motif/love,motif/spring,motif/tears,motif/water,motif/winter,concept/doubt,concept/fear,concept/mercy,concept/revenge,character/duke_of_exeter,character/duke_of_gloucester,character/duke_of_york,character/gloucester,character/henry_vi,character/richard"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Erenter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry and the [[characters/duke-of-exeter\|Duke of Exeter]] |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Here at the palace will I rest a while.<br>Cousin of Exeter, what thinks your lordship?<br>Methinks the power that Edward hath in field<br>Should not be able to encounter mine. |
| EXETER | The [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] is that he will seduce the rest. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | That’s not my [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. My meed hath got me fame.<br>I have not stopped mine ears to their demands,<br>Nor posted off their suits with slow delays.<br>My pity hath been balm to heal their wounds,<br>My mildness hath allayed their swelling griefs,<br>My [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] dried their [[motifs/water\|water]]-flowing [[motifs/tears\|tears]].<br>I have not been desirous of their wealth,<br>Nor much oppressed them with great subsidies,<br>Nor forward of [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]], though they much erred.<br>Then why should they [[motifs/love\|love]] Edward more than me?<br>No, Exeter, these graces challenge grace;<br>And when the [[motifs/lion\|lion]] fawns upon the [[motifs/lamb\|lamb]],<br>The [[motifs/lamb\|lamb]] will never cease to follow him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Shout within ‘A Lancaster’, ‘A York’ |
| EXETER | Hark, hark, my ford—what shouts are these? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward and [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], with soldiers |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Seize on the shame-faced Henry—bear him hence,<br>And once again proclaim us [[archetypes/king\|King]] of England.<br>You are the fount that makes small brooks to flow.<br>Now stops thy [[motifs/spring\|spring]]—my sea shall suck them dry,<br>And swell so much the higher by their ebb.<br>Hence with him to the Tower—tet him not speak. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some with [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry and Exeter |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | And lords, towards Coventry bend we our course,<br>Where peremptory Warwick now remains.<br>The sun shines hot, and, if we use delay,<br>Cold biting [[motifs/winter\|winter]] mars our hoped-for hay. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Away betimes, before his forces join,<br>And take the great-grown traitor unawares.<br>Brave warriors, march amain towards Coventry. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Rientrrano [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico e il [[characters/duke-of-exeter\|duca di Exeter]] |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Qui nel palazzo riposerò un poco.<br>Cugino di Exeter, che pensa vostra signoria?<br>Mi pare che la forza che Edoardo ha in campo<br>non dovrebbe essere in grado d’affrontare la mia. |
| EXETER | Il [[concepts/doubt\|dubbio]] è che egli seduca gli altri. |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] ENRICO | Non è questo il mio [[concepts/fear\|timore]]. Il mio merito m’ha dato fama.<br>Non ho chiuso le mie orecchie alle loro domande,<br>né rinviato le loro istanze con lenti indugi.<br>La mia pietà è stata balsamo a sanare le loro ferite,<br>la mia mitezza ha placato i loro dolori gonfi,<br>la mia [[concepts/mercy\|misericordia]] ha asciugato le loro [[motifs/tears\|lacrime]] fluenti come [[motifs/water\|acqua]].<br>Non sono stato bramoso della loro ricchezza,<br>né li ho molto oppressi con grandi tributi,<br>né pronto alla [[concepts/revenge\|vendetta]], sebbene molto errassero.<br>Perché dunque dovrebbero [[motifs/love\|amare]] Edoardo più di me?<br>No, Exeter, queste grazie reclamano grazia;<br>e quando il [[motifs/lion\|leone]] fa carezze all’[[motifs/lamb\|agnello]],<br>l’[[motifs/lamb\|agnello]] non cesserà mai di seguirlo. |
| *(didascalia)* | Grida dall’interno ‘Lancaster’, ‘York’ |
| EXETER | Udite, udite, mio fignore—che grida son queste? |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[archetypes/king\|re]] Edoardo e [[characters/richard\|Riccardo]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|duca di Gloucester]], con soldati |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | Afferrate il vergognoso Enrico—portatelo via,<br>e ancora una volta proclamateci [[archetypes/king\|re]] d’Inghilterra.<br>Tu sei la fonte che fa scorrere i piccoli ruscelli.<br>Ora si arresta la tua [[motifs/spring\|sorgente]]—il mio mare li succhierà a secco,<br>e si gonfierà tanto più alto col loro riflusso.<br>Via con lui alla Torre—non tasciatelo parlare. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono alcuni con [[archetypes/king\|re]] Enrico ed Exeter |
| [[archetypes/king\|RE]] EDOARDO | E, signori, volgiamo il nostro corso verso Coventry,<br>dove ora sta il perentorio Warwick.<br>Il sole scalda, e, se indugiamo,<br>il freddo [[motifs/winter\|inverno]] pungente guasterà il fieno che speriamo. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICCARDO]] DI [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Via presto, prima che le sue forze si congiungano,<br>e cogliamo alla sprovvista il traditore ormai cresciuto.<br>Bravi guerrieri, marciate a tutta forza verso Coventry. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_5--scene_1" data-title="Richard Duke of York (3 Henry VI) — Act V, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,motif/blood,motif/city,motif/dust,motif/love,motif/nature,motif/rose,motif/time,motif/well,motif/wind,concept/body,concept/doubt,concept/faith,concept/honour,concept/mercy,concept/nature,setting/oxford,character/clarence,character/duke_of_gloucester,character/duke_of_york,character/earl_of_warwick,character/gloucester,character/henry_vi,character/richard"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]], the Mayar of Coventry, two Messengers, and others upon the walls |
| WARWICK | Where is the post that came from valiant [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | The First Messenger steps forward |
| WARWICK | How far hence is thy lord, mine honest fellow? |
| FIRST MESSENGER | By this at Dunsmore, marching hitherward. |
| WARWICK | How far off is our brother Montague?<br>Where is the post that came from Montague? |
| *(didascalia)* | the Second Messenger steps forward |
| SECOND MESSENGER | By this at Da’ntry, with a puissant troop. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Somerville to them, abovel |
| WARWICK | Say, Somerville-what says my loving son?<br>And, by thy guess, how nigh is [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] now? |
| SOMERVILLE | At Southam I did leave him with his forces,<br>And do expect him here some two hours hence.<br>A march afar off |
| WARWICK | Then [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] is at hand-I hear his drum. |
| SOMERVILLE | It is not his, my lord. Here Southam lies.<br>The drum your [[concepts/honour\|honour]] hears marcheth from Warwick. |
| WARWICK | Who should that be? Belike, unlooked-for friends. |
| SOMERVILLE | They are at hand, and you shall quickly know. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter below [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward and [[characters/richard\|Richard]] [[characters/duke-of-gloucester\|Duke of Gloucester]], with soldiers |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Go, trumpet, to the walls, and sound a parley. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound a parley |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | See how the surly Warwick mans the wall. |
| WARWICK | O, unbid spite—is sportful Edward come?<br>Where slept our scouts, or how are they seduced,<br>That we could hear no news of his repair? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Now, Warwick, wilt thou ope the [[motifs/city\|city]] gates,<br>Speak gentle words, and humbly bend thy knee,<br>Call Edward [[archetypes/king\|king]], and at his hands beg [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]]?<br>And he shall pardon thee these outrages. |
| WARWICK | Nay, rather, wilt thou draw thy forces hence,<br>Confess who set thee up and plucked thee down,<br>Call Warwick patron, and be penitent?<br>And thou shalt still remain the [[characters/duke-of-york\|Duke of York]]. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I thought at least he would have said ‘the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’.<br>Or did he make the jest against his will? |
| WARWICK | Is not a dukedom, sir, a goodly gift? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, by my [[concepts/faith\|faith]], for a poor earl to give.<br>I’ll do thee service for so good a gift. |
| WARWICK | ’Twas I that gave the kingdom to thy brother. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Why then, ’tis mine, if but by Warwick’s gift. |
| WARWICK | Thou art no Atlas for so great a weight;<br>And, weakling, Warwick takes his gift again;<br>And Henry is my [[archetypes/king\|king]], Warwick his subject. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | But Warwick’s [[archetypes/king\|king]] is Edward’s prisoner,<br>And, gallant Warwick, do but answer this:<br>What is the [[concepts/body\|body]] when the head is off? |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alas, that Warwick had no more forecast,<br>But whiles he thought to steal the single ten,<br>The [[archetypes/king\|king]] was slyly fingered from the deck. |
| *(didascalia)* | To Warwick You left poor Henry at the Bishop’s palace, |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And ten to one you’ll meet him in the Tower. |
| *(didascalia)* | ’Tis even so—to Warwick yet you are Warwick still. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Come, Warwick, take the [[motifs/time\|time]]—kneel down, kneel<br>down.<br>Nay, when? Strike now, or else the iron cools. |
| WARWICK | I had rather chop this hand off at a blow,<br>And with the other fling it at thy face,<br>Than bear so low a sail to strike to thee. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Sail how thou canst, have [[motifs/wind\|wind]] and tide thy friend,<br>This hand, fast wound about thy coal-black hair,<br>Shall, whiles thy head is warm and new cut off,<br>Write in the [[motifs/dust\|dust]] this sentence with thy [[motifs/blood\|blood]]:<br>‘[[motifs/wind\|Wind]]-changing Warwick now can change no more’. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], with a drummer and soldiers bearing colours |
| WARWICK | O cheerful colours! See where [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] comes. |
| [[settings/oxford\|OXFORD]] | [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], for Lancaster! |
| *(didascalia)* | [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]] and his men pass over the stage and exeunt into the [[motifs/city\|city]] |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward)<br>The gates are open—let us enter too. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | So other foes may set upon our backs?<br>Stand we in good array, for they no [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]<br>Will issue out again and bid us battle.<br>If not, the [[motifs/city\|city]] being but of small defence,<br>We’ll quickly rouse the traitors in the same. |
| *(didascalia)* | WARWICK to [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]], within |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | O, welcome, [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]—for we want thy help. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Marquis of Montague with a drummer and soldiers bearing colours |
| MONTAGUE | Montague, Montague, for Lancaster! |
| *(didascalia)* | Montague and his men pass over the stage and exeunt into the [[motifs/city\|city]] |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Thou and thy brother both shall bye this treason<br>Even with the dearest [[motifs/blood\|blood]] your bodies bear. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | The harder matched, the greater victory.<br>My mind presageth happy gain and conquest. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Somerset with a drummer and soldiers bearing colours |
| SOMERSET | Somerset, Somerset, for Lancaster! |
| *(didascalia)* | Somerset and his men pass over the stage and exeunt into the [[motifs/city\|city]] |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Two of thy name, both dukes of Somerset,<br>Have sold their lives unto the house of York—<br>And thou shalt be the third, an this sword hold. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter George Duke of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] with a drummer and soldiers bearing colours |
| WARWICK | And lo, where George of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] sweeps along,<br>Of force enough to bid his brother battle;<br>With whom an upright zeal to right prevails<br>More than the [[concepts/nature\|nature]] of a brother’s [[motifs/love\|love]]. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], for Lancaster! |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Et tu, Brute—wilt thou stab Caesar too?<br>(To a trumpeter) A parley, sirra, to George of [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound a parley. [[characters/richard\|Richard]] of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]] and George of |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] whisper together |
| WARWICK | Come, [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]], come—thou wilt if Warwick call. |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Father of Warwick, know you what this means? |
| *(didascalia)* | He takes his red [[motifs/rose\|rose]] out of his hat and throws it at Warwick |
| GEORGE OF [[characters/clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Look—here I throw my infamy at thee!<br>I will not ruinate my father’s house,<br>Who gave his [[motifs/blood\|blood]] to lime the stones together,<br>And set up Lancaster. Why, trowest thou, Warwick,<br>That [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]] is so harsh, so blunt, unnatural,<br>To bend the fatal instruments of war<br>Against his brother and his lawful [[archetypes/king\|king]]?<br>Perhaps thou wilt object my holy oath.<br>To keep that oath were more impiety<br>Than Jephthah, when he sacrificed his daughter.<br>I am so sorry for my trespass made<br>That, to deserve [[motifs/well\|well]] at my brothers’ hands,<br>I here proclaim myself thy mortal foe,<br>With resolution, wheresoe’er I meet thee—<br>As I will meet thee, if thou stir abroad—<br>To plague thee for thy foul misleading me.<br>And so, proud-hearted Warwick, I defy thee,<br>And to my brothers turn my blushing cheeks.<br>(To [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward)<br>Pardon me, Edward—I will make amends.<br>(To [[characters/richard\|Richard]])<br>And, [[characters/richard\|Richard]], do not frown upon my faults,<br>For I will henceforth be no more unconstant. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Now welcome more, and ten times more beloved,<br>Than if thou never hadst deserved our hate. |
| [[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] OF [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to George)<br>Welcome, good [[characters/clarence\|Clarence]]—this is brother-like. |
| WARWICK | (to George)<br>O, passing traitor—perjured and unjust! |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | What, Warwick, wilt thou leave the town and fight?<br>Or shall we beat the stones about thine ears? |
| *(didascalia)* | WARWICK aside |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Alas, I am not cooped here for defence.<br><br>(To [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward)<br>I will away towards Barnet presently,<br>And bid thee battle, Edward, if thou dar’st. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | Yes, Warwick—Edward dares, and leads the way.<br>Lords, to the Held—Saint George and victory! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt below [[archetypes/king\|King]] Edward and his company. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] EDWARD | March. The [[characters/earl-of-warwick\|Earl of Warwick]] and his company<br>descend and follow |
